# Replay Clips Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Draw the six demo animations when the site is built, as H.264 clips with posters in both themes, from the committed replay archives; switch the pages and the provenance prose to them; lower the 2D demo's true mass to 0.10; and remove the committed GIFs, leaving the demo commands and the public names to pull request 4.

**Architecture:** `clocks._video` turns raw frames into an H.264 file and reads one back; it imports no Matplotlib. The `clocks._clip_*` modules build, from a replay and a palette, a figure with fixed axes whose artists are set to one archive row at a time; `clocks._clips.render_clip` walks a replay's shots through the encoder. `clocks._site_media.render_site_media`, called by a three-line Quarto pre-render script, stages and publishes twelve clips and twelve posters in the git-ignored `site/media/`, which it alone owns; pages embed them as raw-HTML blocks that the page-source guard pins whole and the rendered-site check decodes.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.12+, NumPy, Matplotlib 3.10 (declared `>=3.9`), FFmpeg through `imageio-ffmpeg` (a development dependency) or `ffmpeg` on `PATH`, pytest, uv, ruff, Quarto 1.11.9 on the runner (1.9.32 on the planning machine), GitHub Actions.

**Spec:** docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation-design.md

**Status:** Draft for review. Revised after Codex xhigh round 1 at `1c3a6b5` (NOT YET: five Blocking, eight Important), round 2 at `906eb19` (NOT YET: three Blocking, eight Important, two Minor), round 3 at `c292181` (NOT YET: no Blocking, five Important, three Minor) round 4 at `84c4959` (NOT YET: one Blocking, four Important, two Minor) and round 5 at `6b14c26`, the first with two reviewers under the four-round escalation rule: Codex xhigh (NOT YET: no Blocking, two Important, two Minor) and Gemini (`gemini-4-neon-xhigh`, an independent read; NOT YET: three Blocking, five Important, four Minor, each checked against the code before it was applied) and round 6 at `c05ef00`, both reviewers again: Codex xhigh (NOT YET: no Blocking, two Important, three Minor; its round-5 request for a Gemini deployment verdict withdrawn) and Gemini (`gemini-4-neon-xhigh`; NOT YET: no Blocking, four Important, all expected sets of Task 14, each checked against the tests before it was applied). Codex xhigh round 7: SOUND ENOUGH TO IMPLEMENT at `d705cc8`; final amendment applies its three Minors and Gemini's round-7 table corrections. Pre-flight scan corrections applied by the coordinator after the verdict: F1 to F13, see the review trail. Task 1 probe stopped on Quarto item (c); amended by the coordinator (rendered comparison as Quarto writes it; model labels; two-mass labels), reviewed before Task 2. Amendment reviewed by Codex at 8859134 (NOT YET: one Blocking, three Important); corrected. Second amendment review (Codex and Gemini at fcbb101, NOT YET); corrected. Third (Codex at c94da89: NOT YET, one Important, a missing expected failure in one mutation row, and one Minor); both applied by the coordinator. Codex's Important 1 of round 5, a Gemini deployment verdict before the merge, was not applied, by the coordinator's ruling: for this work the merge gate is Codex's verdict on the reviewed head with green checks, and Gemini reviews only on escalation. By the coordinator's ruling the work is now two pull requests: this plan is pull request 3, and the demo commands, the public names, the version and the removal of the old modules are pull request 4, `docs/plans/2026-11-24-replay-commands.md`.

The spec's item 3 of section 11 is split in two (decision D3): this plan is everything the site needs, and `docs/plans/2026-11-24-replay-commands.md` is the commands and the public names. Sections 4, 5, 8, 9 and 10, "Testing" (the "Clips" and "Site" parts), the mutation rows for clip frames, trace and cloud mutations, the decode check, the rendered-site check, the source guard, the box margin and prose, "Checks by eye", "Risks", "Not doing" and "Where this design is least sure" bind this plan; section 7 binds the other. Read them before any task. Where this plan and the spec disagree the spec wins and the disagreement is a plan bug to report, except for the departures under "Decisions this plan makes", each of which says why. The plans for pull requests 1 and 2 (`docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-data.md`, `docs/plans/2026-11-23-replay-figures.md`) show the conventions this one follows.

**Terms.** A *row* is one index k of an archive's per-observation arrays: row 0 is the prior, row k the state after k observations, row T the last. A *clip* is the animation of one demo in one theme, an MP4 file; its *poster* is a PNG of its final frame. A *shot* is one drawing of the figure and the number of video frames it is written for. A *themed element* is an element of the rendered HTML whose class list holds `light-content` or `dark-content`. An *embed* is the raw-HTML block of a page's source that holds a clip's two `<video>` elements; its *description* is the paragraph the videos name with `aria-describedby`. The *manifest* is the per-page list of figures in `scripts/check_site_figures.py`. *Retired* files are committed media this track removes.

## Global Constraints

Tooling and conduct:

- Python only through uv: `uv run pytest`, `uv run ruff format .`, `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run python ...`. Never bare `python` or `pip`.
- **Tasks 2 to 12 run the same cycle** (Task 1 is a probe, Task 13 a set of checks by eye and Task 14 the mutation run; each says what it does instead), and their steps say only what is particular to each: write the tests of Step 1; run them and see them fail for the stated reason; implement; run them and see them pass; run the full local gate (`uv run ruff format --check .`, `uv run ruff check .`, `uv run pytest -q`, `uv run pytest -q -m slow`) and read its output; then, in a separate command, commit with the task's message and `git push origin claude-replay-clips`. Do not open a pull request (the coordinator does). Begin every git step with an explicit `cd` to the worktree.
- **Every task that changes a page, the site configuration, the rendered-site check or (from Task 10, which switches it on) anything the pre-render step runs also renders the site and runs both checks**, after the gate and before the commit: `cd site && uv run --frozen quarto render && cd ..`, then `uv run --frozen python scripts/check_site_links.py` and `uv run --frozen python scripts/check_site_figures.py`. State the local `quarto --version` in the commit body; the runner uses 1.11.9 (`.github/workflows/site-check.yml`, `site.yml`).
- Commit messages are passed with a quoted heredoc (`<<'MSG'`) and end with a `Co-Authored-By:` trailer naming the model that wrote the commit.
- Implementers of Tasks 1 to 13 never use `git checkout`, `git restore` or `git stash` to undo anything: a wrong edit is undone by editing. Task 14's mutants are run by the coordinator's harness in separate worktrees.
- **A red step fails on behaviour, not on a missing name.** Where a task's tests import names that do not exist yet, its Step 2 first adds those names with the signatures of its Interfaces block and bodies that do nothing useful (as each task says), so the run shows the assertions failing. A helper that a test merely uses (`_box_margins` in Task 11, the fixtures of Task 7) is not staged this way: it is written whole in Step 1. The guard helpers of Task 9 are the thing under test there, and are staged like library code.
- **The repository is public.** No committed file may contain an absolute local path, a person's name or an email address. This includes probe scripts, contact sheets (their metadata and their pixels are checked) and commit bodies; the one exception is the `Co-Authored-By:` trailer each commit must end with, which names a model, not a person. Working directories, `sys.prefix` and executable paths are printed at run time for a log and never written into a committed file.
- **A long job runs in the foreground** with `timeout: 600000`; if it needs longer, wait in a bounded foreground loop, `until ! pgrep -f '[p]ytest -q' >/dev/null; do sleep 10; done; tail -20 <log>`, and call again; never end a turn on a background job. The bracket in the pattern is load-bearing: the harness runs each call as `zsh -c "<command>"`, so an unbracketed pattern matches the loop's own shell and never exits.

The design's values, copied; a task that needs one uses exactly this:

- **Encoding** (section 4): 20 frames per second, `libx264`, `yuv420p`, CRF 20, preset `slow`, `+faststart`, no audio. A frame is drawn once and written as many times as its dwell requires.
- **Pacing** (section 5) at 20 frames per second: the prior holds 1.2 s; observations 1 to 3 hold 0.9 s; 4 to 8, 0.5 s; 9 to 20, 0.25 s; later ones 0.1 s; the last 2.5 s. That is 14.8 s for 50 observations and 17.8 s for 80: 296 and 356 frames.
- **Canvas** (sections 4 and 5): 750 CSS pixels wide; declared heights `demo_1d` 450, `demo_2d` 450, `demo_multi_mass` 500, `demo_multi_mass_2d` 550, `demo_model_comparison` 420, `demo_echolocation_3d` 550. A frame and a poster are 1500 pixels by twice the declared height. Source Sans 3 through `style(theme)`; constrained layout off; every axes rectangle and limit computed once from the archive and fixed for the clip.
- **Status line** (section 5): top left, "Before any observation", then "After k observations". Direct labels, no legend boxes.
- **Colour roles** (section 5): particles are `belief` dots of one size and opacity; a second mass is `belief_alt`, drawn with a different marker and labelled; the truth is `truth`; clocks and observed data are `ink`; the head wireframe is `muted`; the model-comparison lines are `ordered[0..2]`. No clip has a threshold, so none has a dashed line. No library drawing code contains a literal colour: every colour is a field of the `Palette` passed in, a blend of `background` and `ink`, or fully transparent (`"none"`, or alpha 0).
- **Traces** (section 5): one panel per parameter: mean, a band of one standard deviation, the truth as a solid line, x from 0 to T. The header gives the name, "true 1.50" and "1.42 ± 0.11". The y-limits are those of `clocks._figures.trace_limits`, which leaves the prior row out.
- **Ellipse** (section 5): drawn from `cov` at two standard deviations and labelled "covariance, 2 s.d.".
- **Zoom inset** (section 5), for one-mass clouds: a fixed window centred on the truth, with half-width 1.25 × (final offset from the truth + two final standard deviations) per axis, labelled with its magnification.
- **Camera** (section 5): with F video frames before the final hold, frame f has azimuth −75° + 40° × (1 − cos(π f / (F − 1))) / 2 and elevation 18°; the camera is still during the hold.
- **Renderers never call the clock-rate forward model** (section 1). No module added here imports `clocks.physics`, `clocks.inference`, `clocks.api` or `clocks._scenarios`.
- **Media files** (section 4): `site/media/<demo>.<theme>.mp4` and `site/media/<demo>.<theme>.png`, git-ignored, listed in `_quarto.yml` under `project.resources` as `media/*.mp4` and `media/*.png`, written by `site/prerender.py` named under `project.pre-render`. **`site/media/` belongs wholly to the pre-render step**: nothing else writes there, and the rendered-site check requires the output's `media/` to hold exactly the expected files.
- **Markup** (section 4): each `<video>` carries `class`, `aria-label`, `aria-describedby`, `src`, `poster`, `width="750"`, `height`, `controls`, `playsinline`, `preload="none"`; no `autoplay`, no `loop`, no script. The design's rule `figure.replay video { max-width: 100%; height: auto; }` is the whole of a new file, `site/replay.css`, listed in `_quarto.yml` after `styles.css` (decision D45).
- **Encoder lookup** (section 4): `imageio_ffmpeg`, then `ffmpeg` on `PATH`, then an error with instructions. The library depends on neither.
- **The commands are not touched here.** Until pull request 4 the seven `demo-*` commands keep their present path, options and output: six write GIFs through `clocks._animate`, `demo-density` writes its PNG. Every sentence this plan adds or keeps about them says so, and no task here uses a name or an option that only pull request 4 creates.
- **The 2D truth** (section 8): `TRUE_M` in `demo_2d.py` goes from 0.15 to 0.10. Every shipped continuous truth value lies inside its prior box by at least 5% of the box's width.
- **Nothing computed is compared exactly across machines.** A value computed in floating point is compared at `rtol=1e-12` or looser (with `atol=1e-12` near zero); a literal is compared exactly. **No golden encoded bytes**: no test or check compares a clip, a poster or a figure with a stored copy or a stored hash, and none asserts a clip's size in bytes or its bit rate ("Not doing"); encoded bytes differ between FFmpeg builds (Q2). Two files made in one build are compared with each other where the design asks it (a light clip must differ from its dark one), and pixels are compared only between two renders made in the same process.
- **The archives other than `demo_2d.npz` are not re-recorded** unless Task 1 hits its stop condition. At the end, `git diff --stat main -- assets/replays` lists `demo_2d.npz` only.

**The clip artists' vocabulary.** Tests find axes and artists by gid, so these names are an interface of every clip task. Axes: `"cloud"` (an (x, M) plane), `"map"` (an (x, y) plane), `"strip"` (the clocks under a cloud panel), `"slowing"` (the per-clock panel), `"scene"` (the 3D close view), `"overview"` (the whole prior box), `"fit"` (observed against fitted), `"posterior"` (model probabilities), `"trace-<name>"` with `<name>` from `meta["param_names"]`, and `"inset"` (or `"inset-1"`, `"inset-2"`), which is a child of its panel and is reached through `ax.child_axes`. Artists, with `-1` and `-2` suffixes where a clip has two masses: `"dots"`, `"truth"`, `"ellipse"`, `"clocks"`, `"trace-mean"`, `"trace-band"`, `"trace-truth"`; the figure-level text `"status"`. Each task adds the names particular to its clip.

## Review Focus

Inputs and failure modes the design implies but spells out no test for, most likely to bite first. Each has a test in the task that owns the code.

1. **The layout engine comes back after the first frame.** Under `style(theme)`, `figure.constrained_layout.use` is on. A figure made with `layout="none"` has no engine, and Matplotlib 3.10.8 restores "no engine" after a save by reading that setting again, so the figure has a `ConstrainedLayoutEngine` from its second frame on and every later save warns (measured, Q3). A figure given a placeholder engine with `fig.set_layout_engine("none")` after creation keeps it. Task 3 (`new_figure`) tests the engine after three frames, under warnings as errors.
2. **A one-observation, one-particle run.** `tests/test_cli.py` runs every command at one observation and sixteen particles, pull request 4's wheel check will run two observations, and `--particles 1` is accepted. With T = 1 the dwell list is `[24, 50]` and a trace has one row after the prior; with one particle every standard deviation is 0, so the ellipse degenerates to a point and the zoom window, by the design's rule, has zero width wherever the estimate sits on the truth. Tasks 2 to 6.
3. **The encoder is missing, or dies part-way, while an older clip and poster are in place.** A clip must be whole or absent under its final name; an existing file must survive a failed encode; a rebuilt pair must never be a new clip beside an old poster that looks fresh; and a missing encoder must stop a site build before anything is removed. A failing encoder shows up first as `BrokenPipeError` on a frame write (measured, Q2). Tasks 2 and 8.
4. **A truncated clip decodes without an error.** Cut to half its bytes, a clip keeps its `ftyp` and `moov` boxes, its stream line, exit status 0 and a progress report that ends `progress=end`; only the decoded frame count changes, 129 of 296 (measured, Q2 and Q13; the design's M10 found 97). The decode check must count frames; it must tell a decoder that failed from one that decoded too few; and it must fail loudly when a build's output cannot be parsed. Tasks 2 and 7.
5. **Clocks whose running mean of slowing is not positive, and a close-view row with dots on both sides of the cube.** A reading above 1 makes a clock's running mean of 1 − rate negative early in a run; an unclipped blend then gives a colour outside the `background`-to-`ink` segment, or an RGBA value below 0, which Matplotlib rejects. And if no clock's running mean is ever positive, the scale's largest value is zero or negative: dividing by it fails or turns the ordering round. A valid replay can hold either; no committed archive does (measured, Q8), so only constructed rows reach them. Likewise the small recordings have no row with dots both inside and outside the close view (Q9); the committed prior row has 2 of 500 inside. Tasks 3 and 6.

## Decisions this plan makes

Each is something the spec leaves open, or states in a way that conflicts with the code or with itself. The reason follows, then what it costs if wrong.

**Order and size**

- **D1. The site switches before the commands do.** Section 11 lists the commands before the embeds. In that order the Reproducibility page's sentence that each committed GIF "is the packaged default output of its `uv run demo-*` command" is false from the command task until the switch, and the committed 2D GIF disagrees with its command from the truth change on. Here the clips are built (Tasks 2 to 6), the site switches (7 to 10) and the 2D truth changes (11); the commands and the public names change afterwards, in pull request 4. Every pinned sentence is true at its own commit. Cost if wrong: none to the result; the tasks are the design's.
- **D2. The 2D truth changes after the switch, not before.** The design's section 8 says the change touches "the clip on the landing page and Into the Plane", which exist only after the switch. For one commit (Task 10) the 2D clip and its description show the run at 0.15, honestly; Task 11 re-records and the description test, which reads the archive, forces the numbers to follow. Cost: the 2D description is edited twice.
- **D3. Two pull requests where section 11 has one.** Section 11 says "Three pull requests"; its third is split here, after Task 13, by the coordinator's ruling on the planner's and the reviewer's recommendation. This plan (pull request 3) carries everything the site needs: the encoder, the six clips, the check, the pre-render step, the embeds, the 2D truth, the protocol wording, the checks by eye and a mutation run of its own. `docs/plans/2026-11-24-replay-commands.md` (pull request 4) carries the demo commands, the public names, the version, the upgrade note and the removal of `_animate.py` and the panel modules. The reason is size and review: one pull request would hold sixteen tasks and some two hundred mutants over two unrelated surfaces. The design's two conditions hold at the boundary: the site is correct, and no renderer is unused (every clip module is called by the site's build; `_animate.py` is still called by the commands). What the boundary costs: for the time between the two merges the repository has two animation code paths, and the commands write GIFs in the old layout while the site shows clips; the README and the Reproducibility page say exactly that (Task 10), pinned by tests that pull request 4 removes with the sentences. The earliest possible boundary was after Task 10; it would have left the 2D truth and the browser checks to the other side.
- **D4. Task 1 draws with throwaway code and times on the runner through a temporary workflow.** The design wants every clip drawn and timed, a clean build on Quarto 1.11.9 and cold-runner timings before any layout number is final. The probe script is committed under `docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/` (where pull request 2 put its contact-sheet script) so the runner can run it; `.github/workflows/clip-probe.yml` runs it on a push, and exists only on a throwaway branch that is deleted in the same task: a new workflow cannot be started by hand until it exists on `main`, the gated workflows do not run on a draft, and a fifth file under `.github/workflows/` on this branch would fail the test that ties the Reproducibility page's list to that directory. Its run is found by branch and full commit SHA, which needs no workflow file on `main`. The probe script itself is deleted in Task 13, when the contact sheets are drawn by the real builders. Cost: the drawing is written twice; the gain is that tests are written against a layout that has been looked at.
- **D5. The format's stop condition is stated, and a second version is a plan amendment, not an improvisation.** If the stop condition fires, work stops and the coordinator amends the plan, with review, before any later task. Task 1, Step 7 lists what that amendment must settle (what the loader does with a version-1 file, the version tests whose invalid case is 2 today, every consumer of the archives, and the constraints that assume only `demo_2d.npz` changes) and settles none of it in advance.

**Modules and interfaces**

- **D6. Module layout.** `src/clocks/_video.py` (frames to a file and back, no Matplotlib, so the rendered-site check can import it cheaply); `_clip_parts.py` (the figure, status line, traces, dots, ellipse, inset); `_clip_maps.py` (`demo_2d`, `demo_multi_mass_2d`); `_clip_tracks.py` (`demo_1d`, `demo_multi_mass`); `_clip_models.py` (`demo_model_comparison`); `_clip_echo.py` (`demo_echolocation_3d`); `_clips.py` (the registry, `shots`, `final_frame`, `render_clip`); `_site_media.py` (`render_site_media`). `frame_rgba` joins `save_png` in `_style.py`, sharing its settings. The split keeps each file under the repository's 500-line guideline and puts the two clips that share panels together. Cost: eight small modules where the design names one.
- **D7. `render_clip(replay, path, *, theme="light", encoder=None) -> int`** is the public name section 7 lists; the design gives no signature. It returns the number of frames written. `Clip` and `Shot` are private.
- **D8. Clouds are marker-only lines, not scatter collections.** `Line2D.get_data()` and `Line3D.get_data_3d()` are public and return the plotted coordinates; a 3D scatter has no public getter for its points. The design asks for dots "of one size and opacity", which a line's markers are. Cost: no per-dot size, which no clip needs.
- **D9. The ellipse is a closed 121-point line**, `mean + 2·(columns of the covariance's eigen-decomposition)·(cos t, sin t)`. A test can then state the property itself: every vertex is at Mahalanobis distance 2 from the mean under the archive's covariance block. An `Ellipse` patch would be tested through its width, height and angle, which a wrong but self-consistent decomposition satisfies.
- **D10. The decode check parses FFmpeg's own report, and reads the process's result first.** `imageio-ffmpeg` ships no `ffprobe` (design, M10). `probe` runs the encoder as a decoder with `-f null - -progress pipe:1 -nostats`. A non-zero exit status is a failed decode whatever was printed; then the codec, pixel format, size and frame rate come from the input's `Video:` line and the frame count from the last record of a progress report that must end `progress=end`; the box order comes from the file itself. Two builds give the same line and report here (Q2, Q13), and Task 1 runs the same small clips through the Linux binary on the runner. A line or report the patterns do not match raises with the text, so a differently worded build fails the check instead of passing it. Cost: a future FFmpeg that rewords either breaks the check loudly until the pattern is widened.
- **D11. A test that needs the encoder fails when there is none; it is not skipped.** The design's "Testing" says so. Where the encoder is: CI's default test step installs the development group (`uv sync --locked`), so the real-encoder tests run there; its slow step selects only tests marked slow, none of which encodes; the site check and the deploy install the same group (`uv sync --frozen`) and run the pre-render step and the checker; the replay report records and compares archives and encodes nothing. A contributor who installs only the wheel and runs the suite sees failures that name the encoder; pull request 4's wheel check shows that the commands themselves work without it. If the Linux wheel's binary does not run (the design's untested risk), Task 1 finds out on the runner before anything depends on it.
- **D12. Frames go to the encoder as RGBA**, the bytes `savefig(format="rgba")` writes, with `-pix_fmt rgba` on the input: no conversion in Python. They are the pixels `save_png` writes (measured equal, Q3).
- **D13. Site media are staged, then published as pairs** (the coordinator's ruling). `clocks._video.encode` writes to a temporary name beside its destination and moves it into place, so a clip is whole or absent. `render_site_media` goes further for a clip and its poster together: both are first written under staged names (the final names with the prefix `.staged-`); only when both exist are the two old files removed and the two new ones moved in. A failure while staging, the encoder's included, leaves the old pair exactly as it was. An interruption during the few renames of publication leaves a file missing, which the next run rebuilds and the rendered-site check fails on; it never leaves a new clip beside an old poster that looks fresh. Cost: for an instant during publication a pair is absent, and a clip is written twice over (temporary, staged, final) for the sake of one rule everywhere.
- **D14. `site/media/` belongs to the pre-render step, and a full render leaves only current media in it** (the coordinator's ruling). The design says a full render "deletes anything else in `site/media/`". A full render removes every `.mp4` and `.png` directly inside the directory that it did not just write; every run first removes leftovers of its own staging, recognised by the `.staged-` prefix and by nothing else (a `.tmp` file there is not claimed); it never recurses and never touches another suffix. Only `media/*.mp4` and `media/*.png` are resources, so nothing else there can be published. Two limits are stated where they apply: a render that is not full keeps every other clip and poster, so only a full render followed by the check, which requires the output's `media/` to hold exactly the expected files, establishes the published set; and two runs at once in one directory are not supported.
- **D15. A clip is rebuilt unless both of its files are strictly newer than its archive and every source file.** The design says "older than"; on a file system with coarse timestamps an edit and a render can share one, and rebuilding on a tie is the safe side. **Superseded after the pull request's review, round 4** (the coordinator's ruling, after four rounds each found another timestamp race): freshness is by content, never by time. Each pair has an unpublished record, `site/media/<demo>.<theme>.sha256`, holding the SHA-256 of the archive's bytes and every source file's relative path and bytes; a pair is fresh only when its clip, poster and record exist and the record holds exactly the run's digest. An archive replaced while the step runs is caught by the check after the final rename for pairs the run rebuilds, and by the next run for pairs it kept. `clocks._site_media`'s docstring is the current statement.
- **D16. Model-comparison lines are coloured by position in `model_ks`**, and a replay with more than three candidates is refused with `ValueError`: `ordered` has three colours. The limit is stated in the error message and in `render_clip`'s docstring, the public way in. The design's literal fixture has two candidates and must draw.

**Drawing details the design leaves open**

- **D17. "After 1 observation" is singular.** The design writes "After k observations".
- **D18. The zoom label** reads `zoom ×12` when both axes' magnifications round to the same whole number and `zoom ×12 (x), ×9 (M)` otherwise; a per-axis window in an inset of fixed shape has two magnifications. A window whose half-width would be zero (a one-particle run on the truth) takes 1% of that parameter's prior range instead.
- **D19. The slowing panel's limits hold the prior row.** The design leaves the prior out of the *trace* limits and says the slowing panel's `belief` bars "show the prior's prediction" before any observation, so they must be in view. This is not Task 1's to reverse: if the prior's bars flatten the rest beyond reading, Task 1 stops, and a clipping rule is adopted only after the design is amended.
- **D20. Layout rectangles are data, fixed by Task 1.** Each clip module holds one `LAYOUT` mapping of axes gid to a rectangle in figure fractions. Tests assert that the rectangles lie on the canvas, do not overlap and never move; they do not pin the numbers, which are a matter for the eye.
- **D21. Descriptions use the trace headers' decimals** (`clocks._figures.trace_decimals`: two for positions, three for masses), so a description, a header and a poster agree digit for digit. The descriptions' numbers are checked against the archive actually committed, the 0.10 run from Task 11 on, not against this plan's copies of them.

**The site**

- **D22. An embed is a fenced raw block**, ```` ```{=html} ````, not bare HTML, so the guard can pin the whole block, in the source, as one string built from a table. The page Quarto writes is not that block byte for byte (measured on 1.11.9 and 1.9.32 alike, Task 1's "Probe results", item (c)): Quarto's HTML post-processing adds the class `figure` to the `<figure>`, which becomes `class="replay figure"`, and writes the boolean attributes `controls` and `playsinline` as `controls=""` and `playsinline=""`; every other attribute keeps its value and its order, the `src` and `poster` paths are untouched and the `<figcaption>` is unchanged. **The rendered page is therefore checked as Quarto writes it** (the coordinator's ruling on Task 1's stop): the rendered-site check requires the replay figure's class list to be exactly `replay` and `figure`, so a Quarto upgrade that changes it fails loudly (Quarto is pinned at 1.11.9 on the runner), and counts a `<video>` boolean attribute (`controls`, `playsinline`, `autoplay`, `loop`) as present when its key is there, bare, `=""` or with any value, as HTML does. The source guard reads the source, which keeps the canonical block, and is unchanged. The description is a fenced div, `::: {#demo-2d-text}`, directly after it.
- **D23. The source guard pins each embed whole and bans video markup anywhere else on a page; it does not judge whether the block is live or visible** (the coordinator's ruling after round 2). That is the lesson of pull request 2, learnt a second time here: a hand-written rule about Markdown context drew a new spelling each round (an HTML comment and a hiding div in round 1; a tilde fence and a four-backtick fence in round 2). The guard's limit is written in the test file in the form pull request 2 used. **It catches** an embed's markup changed by mistake: each canonical block must be present exactly once, as one whole string built from a table, in the table's order, followed by its description, with no other video markup on the page. **It does not catch** a canonical block that Markdown does not render (inside a code fence or an HTML comment) or one that renders inside a hiding wrapper. **The rendered-site check does**: a block swallowed by a fence or a comment produces no `<video>` and fails the count of themed elements against the manifest, and a wrapper that carries one of the markers D39 names fails the hidden-ancestor rule; Task 7 has a fixture for each, and Task 14 a mutant. Neither check establishes more than that: a wrapper that hides by a means D39 does not name (the reviewer rendered a canonical block inside a div styled `opacity: 0`, and both checks accept it; an ancestor's `clip-path` is another) is found only in a browser, where Task 13 requires each visible video and its description to be scrolled into view, inside the viewport, under no ancestor of zero opacity and seen painted in a screenshot, and the video to be reachable by tabbing and playable from the keyboard.
- **D24. The landing page and "Into the Plane" carry the same description** of the 2D clip, word for word. Seven embeds, six descriptions.
- **D25. The stylesheet checks apply only when the manifest lists a clip**, so the checker's plot-only fixtures need neither `replay.css` nor a link to it.
- **D26. `scripts/check_site_figures.py` imports `clocks`** (`clocks._video`, `clocks._replay`) for the first time. It already runs under `uv run`, where the package is installed.
- **D27. The check reads each clip's expected frame count from its archive** (`assets/replays/<clip>.npz`, `n_observations`), so the count cannot go stale in a second table. A count proves length, not content: another clip of equal length would pass the stream check. What ties the published files to their inputs is the build, not the check: a full render redraws every clip and poster from `assets/replays/`, a failed pre-render step fails the render (Task 1, item g), a pair is published only after both of its files were staged from one archive in one run (D13), and the check then requires the output's `media/` to hold exactly the expected names, at the intended paths, light differing from dark. Stated limit: the check alone cannot tell that a clip's frames show its archive. Considered and not done: redrawing each poster in the check and comparing it with the published one, which is an image comparison of the kind "Not doing" rules out.

**Commands and names**

- **D28 to D31 are in `docs/plans/2026-11-24-replay-commands.md`** (the commands' options, what each command writes, `demo-density`'s archive, and the removal of the old names), where they are decisions C3 to C8, with the rulings of this round. One part of the old D31 stays here: `render_clip` joins `clocks.viz` in Task 3, when it first exists; adding a name breaks nothing.
- **D32. The README's GIF and its paragraph go in Task 10**, with the file they show, not with the commands: a README image of a deleted file is a broken image, and the retired-asset guard forbids the mention.

**Prose and records**

- **D33. "Run exactly once" becomes a protocol statement in five current-facing places**: the four the design names and `docs/someday-maybe.md`, which repeats the sentence. Three kinds of text keep the phrase. A test requires every document that keeps it to be one of the first kind: historical records, which say what was attested at the time (`docs/2026-08-16-development-calibration.md`, whose lines 18 and 154 to 155 attest the August release; the audit; the earlier specs and plans); `scripts/scan_echolocation_range.py`, whose notice is an instruction printed when a certification run starts, not a claim about the past; and tests.
- **D34. The earlier seed blocks are disclosed, with citations checked by the implementer.** The owner's decision is "reworded as a protocol statement, with the earlier seed blocks disclosed"; the design allows the sentence "only with a citation the implementer has checked". The citations exist in the repository (read while planning): seeds 100–111 used as a holdout and then set aside (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-03-clone-freeze-diagnosis.md`), seeds 200–211 as a certification block (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-02-annealed-jitter-design.md`), seeds 300–311 as the echolocation certification block of 2026-07-19 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-19-3d-echolocation-design.md`).
- **D35. The calibration report keeps its six GIF rows and gains one dated sentence per file**, as pull request 2 did for the two PNGs.
- **D36. The audit's status table** is brought up to date in the task where each thing lands: rows for C6, V1 and V5 in Task 11, the row for C9 and the end of T2's row in Task 12, each with a dated line that lists only the rows that task added. The audit's body is a record and is not edited.
- **D37. Deferred items from pull request 2's review.** `gh pr view 28 --comments` lists two explicitly, both Minor and both in `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`; Task 9 takes them. From the coordinator's list this plan also takes, because their files are edited here: a test that `site_libs/` is skipped and a test of the unknown-kind `ValueError` (both Task 7; both pin present behaviour). Left: label positions in the two static figures, which stay unpinned. They are layout, checked on the contact sheets, and `_figures.py` and `_echo_study.py` are not edited here.

**Decisions added after review** (D13, D14, D23, D25 and D27 above were also rewritten in review)

- **D38. Quarto is told about the pre-render step, and about the media, in Task 10, with the embeds.** Task 8 adds the function, the script and their tests only. Were the `pre-render` and `resources` lines added in Task 8, two commits would build and ship twenty-four files that no page shows, and the check's rule that the output's `media/` holds exactly the expected files would fail on them.
- **D39. The hidden ancestors the rendered-site check refuses** are a named list of markers, not a computation: a class among `d-none`, `hidden`, `invisible`, `visually-hidden`, `collapse`; the attribute `hidden`; `aria-hidden="true"`; an inline `style` holding `display:none` or `visibility:hidden`; the elements `<template>` and `<noscript>`; and a `<details>` element without the attribute `open` (a closed disclosure, which the reviewer rendered around a canonical block: the videos arrive whole inside it). They are looked for on the video, on its replay figure, on any ancestor, and likewise for the description. Opacity, position, clipping (an ancestor's `clip-path` or `overflow`) and anything a stylesheet or a script does are not looked for: the checker does not parse styles beyond those two declarations. Every statement in this plan that the check "refuses a hidden clip" means these markers and no more; a clip hidden some other way is the browser check's to find (Task 13), which the test file says.
- **D40. A poster decodes when Pillow says so** (the coordinator's ruling after round 2): `Image.open` identifies it as PNG, `verify()` passes, and a second open followed by `load()` reads every pixel. The first draft's standard-library walk accepted a correctly checksummed PNG whose scanline filter bytes are invalid (the reviewer's probe; reproduced here, Q15). The checker imports Pillow and not Matplotlib. Because a script under `scripts/` then imports Pillow by name, Task 7 adds `pillow` to the development dependency group and `uv.lock` changes: `pyproject.toml` does not declare it today, and it is in the lock (12.1.1) only as Matplotlib's dependency. Cost: one more declared development dependency, already installed.
- **D41. Two private seams, `_run_encoder` and `_run_decoder`, are the only places `_video.py` starts a process.** Tests of arguments and failure handling replace them with Python stand-ins instead of writing shell scripts, which would tie the suite to POSIX; tests of the stream drive the real encoder. Cost: the stand-ins assume the seam's signature.
- **D42. The probe workflow mirrors the site workflows' setup and logs the interpreter; it does not pin one.** `site-check.yml` and `site.yml` run `uv python install 3.12` and then `uv sync --frozen` without selecting a version, and the repository has no `.python-version` file. The probe exists to time what those jobs will do, so it repeats their steps and prints `sys.version`; if the logged interpreter is not a 3.12, that is a finding reported in "Probe results" for the coordinator (the site workflows would then need `--python 3.12`), not something this plan changes.
- **D43. Two sentences say what the commands still do**, one in the README and one on the Reproducibility page, each pinned by a test, for the time between the two pull requests (D3).
- **D44. The mutation run may be spread over several worktrees**, one mutant at a time in each, with every row's expected set declared before the run and no row dropped (the coordinator's ruling).
- **D45. The responsive rule lives in a file of its own, pinned whole** (the coordinator's ruling after round 3). Section 4 says `site/styles.css` gains the rule. Three rounds each found a spelling a stylesheet matcher accepted (joined whitespace; split declaration tokens; a comment whose removal joins `1/**/00%`), and a rule nested in an at-rule or overridden lower in the file would have been next. So nothing is parsed: `site/replay.css` holds exactly `figure.replay video { max-width: 100%; height: auto; }` and a final newline, `_quarto.yml` lists it in the HTML format's `css` after `styles.css`, a test compares the file's bytes with that text, and the rendered-site check requires the output's `replay.css` to be those bytes and every page with a clip to link it. Task 1 confirms that Quarto 1.11.9 copies a `css:` file byte for byte and links it from a root page and a subdirectory page. Stated limit, in the checker's tests too: another stylesheet or a script can still override the rule; that a clip actually shrinks with a narrow column is established by the 390-pixel browser check of Task 13. Cost: one more small file and one more request per page. One part of the ruling could not be followed as given: it asked that `_quarto.yml`'s css list be read "with the YAML reader the tests already use", and the tests have none (they use `tomllib` for TOML and read workflow files as text; PyYAML is in the lock only as a dependency of Jupyter and is not declared). The test pins the three lines of the `css:` block as text, inside the `format` block's `html` block, as the workflow tests pin theirs, and the rendered link is what shows that Quarto read the list (the reviewer accepted this in round 4). The link check resolves each `href` with `_site_path`, the URL rules of an image's `src` moved out of `_resolve_source` (round 4), so a malformed link, or one whose decoded path holds a NUL or is empty, is not counted as a link to `replay.css`; the page fails, with its one message, only when no `href` counts, and nothing raises.
- **D46. Whether the close view's dots reach the canvas is measured by rendering, not read from `zorder`.** In a 3D axes Matplotlib orders artists at draw time, so an artist's stored `zorder` says little (measured, Q16: an opaque box around the dots, with any `zorder`, hides nothing unless the axes' `computed_zorder` is turned off). The test renders the scene twice in one process, with the dots shown and hidden, and requires the two frames to differ inside the scene's pixel rectangle. `opaque_fills_above` is kept for 2D axes only, where Matplotlib does draw in `zorder` order; there it shows that no opaque filled artist is drawn after the cloud, and it is deliberately coarse: it does not ask whether such an artist overlaps the cloud, and it does not look at child axes.
- **D47. No mutant may hang the mutation run** (the coordinator's ruling after round 4). Each mutant's whole-suite run has a time limit, the harness kills the run's process group when it passes, and a timeout is a sixth class of discrepancy, resolved before the run counts as evidence (Task 14). A row whose edit is known to hang is not declared: the round-3 row "`wait()` before the pipe is closed" stalled every real encode on the successful path, since FFmpeg exits only at end of input, and was replaced by the same reordering on the broken-pipe path, where the encoder is expected to have exited already (measured in round 5: 20 to 31 ms), the time limit covering any other outcome; the successful path's order is pinned by `test_run_encoder_reaps_the_process[success]` and has no mutant.
- **D48. Evidence belongs to one head** (the coordinator's ruling after round 5). A correction of an expected set is recorded in Task 14 itself; a fix to code or tests that the mutation run leads to reopens the task that owns the code, reruns its gates and any browser checks it affects, and restarts the mutation run from a clean baseline at the new head, so no evidence in the hand-over is about an earlier head than the one reviewed.
- **D49. The close view's prior row is not assumed empty** (round 5). The small echolocation recording has no prior dot inside the cube on the machine measured (Q9), but `view_bounds` follows the run's own path, which can differ in low-order bits between processors; so the scene's cloud is listed as shown "when any is in view", and the empty-crop test builds its empty prior row instead of relying on the recording's.
- **D50. The model-comparison labels follow the line heads, with no connectors** (the coordinator's ruling after Task 1; design section 5 amended to match). The design put the labels in a fixed gutter, each joined to its line's head by a thin connector. On the probe's sheet (`clip-demo_model_comparison.jpg`) the connectors cross the whole empty panel at early rows, where they read as projections of the lines, and cross each other at the last row. Each label now sits 6 points right of its head, kept `LABEL_GAP` (12 points) from the next without changing their top-to-bottom order, ties in `model_ks` order (Task 5). Cost: labels move with the heads; the minimum gap is what keeps crossing lines from stacking them.
- **D51. The two-mass labels are placed by a rule, once, from the final row** (the coordinator's ruling after Task 1). Tasks 4 and 5 said "beside its truth"; on the probe's sheets a fixed offset put the labels on the final clouds. `label_masses` takes the probe's rule: eight offsets, (±9, ±7), (±9, 0) and (0, ±7) points, tried in a fixed order, the one covering the fewest final-row marks without leaving the panel or touching an inset or the other label, ties to the earlier. The probe's offsets are 9 points across and 7 up or down, so a diagonal offset is (9, 7), not 7 along each axis. At rows 0 and 1 the labels lie over prior dots, as any label inside a panel full of the prior would (Probe results; Task 1's review flagged it), so each is drawn with a 3-point halo in `background`, which keeps it legible in both themes. Cost: the halo hides the dots just under the text.

## Measurements

Run on 2026-11-24 in the worktree at `177a425`, macOS arm64, Python 3.14.0, NumPy 2.4.2, Matplotlib 3.10.8, through `uv run`. Probe scripts were in a session scratch directory and are not kept. A number in this plan is one of these, or is copied from the design with its section, or is marked believed.

- **Q1. Encoders here.** `uv run --with imageio-ffmpeg python -c "import imageio_ffmpeg as m; print(m.__version__, m.get_ffmpeg_exe())"` resolved 0.8.1 under the project's seven-day cooldown; its binary is `ffmpeg-macos-aarch64-v9.0`, FFmpeg 9.0 built with `--enable-gpl --enable-libx264`. Homebrew's `ffmpeg` on `PATH` is 9.0.2. The Linux binary was not run.
- **Q2. Encoding and decoding.** 296 synthetic frames of 1500 by 900, piped as raw frames with the settings of Global Constraints, through each build: exit 0, 27,454 bytes each, **different bytes** between the two builds; boxes `ftyp`, `moov`, `free`, `mdat`. Decoding either file with either build (`-hide_banner -nostdin -i <file> -map 0:v:0 -f null - -progress pipe:1 -nostats`): the same line, `Stream #0:0[0x1](und): Video: h264 (High) (avc1 / 0x31637661), yuv420p(progressive), 1500x900, 12 kb/s, 20 fps, 20 tbr, 10240 tbn (default)`, and a last progress line `frame=296`. The decoder's standard error holds two `Video:` lines, the input's first and then one for its null output. The file cut to half its bytes: exit 0, the same boxes and stream line, `frame=129`, with `Invalid NAL unit size` on standard error. An output path in a directory that does not exist: the encoder exits and the next frame write raises `BrokenPipeError`. Small clips, both builds: three solid frames of 64 by 48 written 2, 1 and 4 times decode to `frame=7` with the same boxes; copied with `-c copy` and no flag the boxes are `ftyp`, `free`, `mdat`, `moov` and the count is still 7; sixty frames of seeded noise make 117,153 bytes, and the half file decodes to `frame=29` with its stream line intact; a text file named `.mp4` gives exit 183 and no `Video:` line; re-encoded with `-c:v mpeg4`, and with `-c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv444p`, the lines are those quoted in Task 2's parser test. `-version` prints `ffmpeg version ...` as the first line of standard output. Decoded with `-frames:v 1 -f rawvideo -pix_fmt rgb24 -`, the first pixel of a clip whose frames are the light background (249, 245, 215) is (247, 244, 214) in both builds.
- **Q3. Frames.** Inside `style(theme)`: `plt.figure(figsize=(7.5, 4.5), layout="none")` reports no layout engine; after one `savefig` it reports a `ConstrainedLayoutEngine`, and a second save after `add_axes` warns "There are no gridspecs with layoutgrids". `plt.figure(figsize=(7.5, 4.5))` followed by `fig.set_layout_engine("none")` reports a `PlaceHolderLayoutEngine`, the same object after four saves and a draw, with no warning under `warnings.simplefilter("error")` and with a plain axes, an inset and a 3D axes all keeping their positions. `fig.savefig(buffer, format="rgba", dpi=200)` gives 1500 by 840, 900, 1000, 1080 and 1100 pixels for heights of 4.2, 4.5, 5.0, 5.4 and 5.5 inches, every alpha 255, equal pixel for pixel to the file `save_png` writes. A 500-dot figure with two axes redraws and saves in about 20 ms; a 3D axes with 500 dots in about 32 ms. `ax.inset_axes(...)` is in `ax.child_axes` and not in `fig.axes`. A styled canvas with nothing on it has 1 distinct colour; with one empty axes about 218; with a status line about 447.
- **Q4. The dwell schedule**, by the rule of Global Constraints: 296 frames for T = 50 and 356 for T = 80; `[24, 50]` for T = 1; `[24, 18, 50]` for T = 2.
- **Q5. The camera**, by the design's formula with F = 306 (T = 80): frames 0, 76, 152, 229 and 305 have azimuths −75.0, −69.17850576840875, −55.103002582480016, −40.82149423159126 and −35.0. A constant-speed turn would give −65.03 at frame 76. With F = 24 (T = 1): frames 0, 6, 12 and 23 give −75.0, −68.65106286437309, −53.63515173270658 and −35.0.
- **Q6. The 2D run at M = 0.10**, in a scratch clone with `TRUE_M = 0.10` and `scripts/record_replays.py --out assets/replays --overwrite demo_2d`: final mean (1.41692, −1.22229, 0.10002) ± (0.11108, 0.11479, 0.00124), the design's M2; after observation 1, (3.630, −3.361, 0.1225) ± (1.465, 1.502, 0.0152); after 8, (1.861, −1.352, 0.0993) ± (0.304, 0.298, 0.0032). Largest |x–y correlation| after the prior: row 3, −0.690. Running mean of slowing: largest over the run 0.031689, largest in the last row 0.024270, 2 of 400 clock-rows above 1.1 times the latter. Zoom half-widths by the design's rule: 0.38156 (x), 0.56483 (y), 0.0031185 (M). Weak-field strength 0.0468. With that archive, `uv run pytest -q -m '' tests/test_replays.py tests/test_demo_records.py tests/test_shipped_ground_truths.py tests/test_record_replays_script.py`: 237 passed. The "Into the Plane" cell at 0.10: (1.3716, −1.0980, 0.1003) ± (0.1724, 0.1696, 0.0018), largest particle mass 0.1068, 0.162 from the true position; today, at 0.15: (1.4049, −1.0768, 0.1487) ± (0.1114, 0.1140, 0.0010), largest particle mass 0.1500.
- **Q7. `TRUE_M` put back to 0.15 against the 0.10 archive**, whole suite (`uv run pytest -q -m ''`): 3 failed, 1,918 passed. The three: `tests/test_replays.py::test_archive_matches_its_scenario[demo_2d]`, `::test_gate_inputs[demo_2d]`, `::test_gate_summaries[demo_2d]`.
- **Q8. The committed archives**, from `load_replay`. Final mean ± standard deviation at the trace headers' decimals: `demo_1d` (T 50, 1,000 particles, 3 clocks) x 2.48 ± 0.04, M 0.101 ± 0.001; `demo_2d` at 0.15 (T 50, 2,000, 8 clocks) x 1.43 ± 0.07, y −1.15 ± 0.08, M 0.149 ± 0.001; `demo_multi_mass` (T 80, 3,000, 5 clocks) x₁ −2.00 ± 0.04, x₂ 4.55 ± 0.05, M₁ 0.045 ± 0.001, M₂ 0.030 ± 0.001, and after observation 1 x₂ 6.61 ± 1.04, after 8 x₂ 4.62 ± 0.18; `demo_multi_mass_2d` (T 80, 4,000, 10 clocks) x₁ −2.38 ± 0.42, y₁ 2.03 ± 0.22, x₂ 4.13 ± 0.80, y₂ −0.95 ± 0.63, M₁ 0.052 ± 0.004, M₂ 0.028 ± 0.003; `demo_echolocation_3d` (T 80, 6,000, 27 clocks) x 0.98 ± 0.02, y 1.46 ± 0.02, z 2.93 ± 0.03, M 0.078 ± 0.002, `view_bounds` (−1.3, 4.8), `weight_in_view[0]` 0.0045 (printed to one decimal as 0.4%), smallest later value 0.9999999999999998, dots inside the view in rows 0, 1 and 80: 2, 500 and 500. `demo_model_comparison` (T 80, 2,000 per candidate): `model_ks` 1, 2, 3; prior one third each; the leader is K = 3 from observation 1, K = 2 from 10, K = 3 from 19; final (0, 0.39904, 0.60096). Position standard deviations after observation 1 against the prior's: `demo_1d` 0.27 against 4.92; echolocation 0.16 to 0.26 against 9.2 to 9.3. The running mean of slowing is positive in every clock-row of `demo_1d`, `demo_2d` and `demo_multi_mass` (smallest 0.0102); for `demo_2d` at 0.15 its largest value over the run is 0.042842 and its largest in the last row 0.036333, a ratio of 0.848.
- **Q9. Small recordings.** `record(n_observations=9, n_particles=40)` and `record(n_observations=1, n_particles=16)` succeed for all six clip demos under warnings as errors, in 0.02 to 0.18 s each. Echolocation at 9 and 40: `view_bounds` (−1.3, 4.6); dots inside the view per row: 0, then 40 in each later row.
- **Q10. The suite today.** 1,921 tests: 1,913 in the default run, 8 slow. `uv run pytest -q -m ''` took 3 min 19 s.
- **Q11. The project's version is in the lock.** `uv.lock` holds `name = "clocks"`, `version = "0.1.0"`, `source = { editable = "." }`; CI installs with `uv sync --locked`.
- **Q12. What the tree says about GIFs and the old names** (`grep` over the tree outside `docs/plans`, `docs/audits`, `docs/superpowers`, `.venv` and build output). GIF files: six under `assets/`, the same six under `site/assets/`. Embeds: `site/index.qmd` line 24; `site/story/the-search-in-one-dimension.qmd` 11; `into-the-plane.qmd` 23; `two-hidden-masses.qmd` 11 and 13; `how-many-masses.qmd` 15; `gravitational-echolocation.qmd` 108; `README.md` 64. Prose: `README.md` 55 to 61 and 66 to 81; `site/reproduce/getting-started.qmd` 27 to 36; `site/reproduce/reproducibility.qmd` 41, 61 and 87 to 97; `docs/someday-maybe.md` 43; `scripts/check_site_figures.py` 17. Code: the six `OUTPUT_PATH` constants and `demo_model_comparison.py` line 203; `src/clocks/_animate.py`. Tests: `tests/test_generated_assets.py` (the hash table and two sentences), `tests/test_cli.py` (`DEMO_CASES`), `tests/test_viz.py`, and two fixtures in `tests/test_site_figure_style.py` that use `demo_2d.gif` as a name that is not retired. The old `clocks.viz` names are used only by the six demo modules, `tests/test_viz.py` and `clocks.viz` itself; `_animate`, `_panels` and `_panels3d` are also named in `README.md` (lines 108 and 111) and `site/reproduce/architecture.qmd` (lines 29 to 31, 62 to 69 and 89). "Exactly once": `README.md` 88; `site/reproduce/getting-started.qmd` 49; `site/reproduce/reproducibility.qmd` 119; `site/method/the-particle-filter.qmd` 179; `docs/someday-maybe.md` 14; `scripts/scan_echolocation_range.py` 213. No page has a `{=html}` block, a `<figure>` or a `<video>` today. "Exactly once" also stands in historical records: `docs/2026-08-16-development-calibration.md` (lines 18 and 154 to 155, the August release's attestation), the audit, and the earlier specs and plans. The two style-guard fixtures that mention `demo_2d.gif` test against their own one-name list (`_RETIRED = ("demo_density.png",)`), so they stay green whatever the real list holds; Task 9 gives their unrelated image a made-up name.
- **Q13. The decoder's result, as a process.** For the 92-frame fixture clip of Task 7, its half, the sixty-frame noise clip's half and a text file, in both builds: the whole clip exits 0 with one `frame=92` line and `progress=end` as the last line of standard output; each cut clip exits 0, with `frame=45` and `frame=29`, and also ends `progress=end`; the text file exits 183 with no standard output. So on these builds a cut file is a finished decode with a short count, and only the count shows it. The reviewer, with Quarto 1.9.32's Pandoc, rendered a canonical embed wrapped in `::: {.d-none}`: the videos and the description came out whole under `<div class="d-none">` (not re-run here).
- **Q14. Covariance blocks of the recordings the suite draws.** For `record(n_observations, n_particles)` at (1, 16), (2, 16) and (9, 40), over every row and every block an ellipse is drawn from, the smallest eigenvalue divided by the block's trace is at least 9.1e-6 (`demo_multi_mass`; 3.3e-5 for `demo_1d`, 0.12 or more for the two map demos): positive definite, far from rounding. Only the one-particle recordings have a block with no spread.
- **Q15. What Pillow 12.1.1 makes of damaged PNGs.** `verify()` and, on a fresh open, `load()`, on files built with `struct` and `zlib` (2 by 2 RGB) and on a 1500 by 900 poster written by Matplotlib: a whole file passes both; scanlines with filter byte 9 pass `verify()` and fail `load()` (`OSError`), as does image data that inflates to 3 bytes; a file ending before its `IEND` chunk fails `verify()` (`OSError`) and passes `load()`; a flipped byte in `IDAT` fails both (`SyntaxError`, `OSError`); a file cut in half fails both; a file cut to its first 33 bytes, the signature and `IHDR`, and four bytes of text, are not identified as images at all (`UnidentifiedImageError`). So both steps are needed, and each has a fixture only it catches. `pyproject.toml` has no entry for Pillow; `uv.lock` has `pillow` 12.1.1.
- **Q16. Round 3's probes**, at `c292181`. A 3D axes on a 7.5 by 5.5 inch figure, its limits the committed echolocation archive's `view_bounds`, elevation 18 and azimuth −35, holding as a marker-only `Line3D` the dots of a row that lie inside the view; two frames at 200 dpi, with the line shown and hidden. Pixels that differ, all of them inside the axes' window extent: with marker size 3, 242 at row 0 (2 dots), 7,194 at row 1 and 463 at row 80 (500 dots each); with marker size 1, 74, 4,994 and 256. With the axes' `computed_zorder` set to false and an opaque `Poly3DCollection` of the view cube's six faces in the background colour at `zorder=10`: 0 in every case, both themes, and the axes' limits unchanged. With `computed_zorder` left on, an opaque box round two dots changed nothing (still 242), whatever its `zorder`: the stored value does not order a 3D draw. Repeated with each row at the azimuth of its first shot, as `test_echo_close_view_dots_reach_the_canvas` shows it (row 0 at −75, row 1 at −74.39, row 80 at −35), marker size 1, alpha 0.5, the dots in `belief`, both themes: 74, 3,950 and 211 pixels differ, none outside the axes' window extent; with the occluding box, 0 (at marker size 1 for row 0 and 3 for row 1). `MarkerStyle(m).is_filled()` is false for `"_"`, `"|"`, `"+"` and `"x"` and true for `"o"`, `"s"`, `"^"` and `"D"`. `Image.open` of a directory raises `IsADirectoryError`, an `OSError`. `site/_quarto.yml` has `css: styles.css`, a single value, under `format: html:`. No test or script imports a YAML reader; `uv.lock` has `pyyaml` and `pyproject.toml` does not name it. The worktree has no rendered `site/_output`, so how Quarto links a `css:` file was not looked at here (Task 1, item f).
- **Q17. Round 4's probes**, at `84c4959`, Matplotlib 3.10.8. A `Line3DCollection` of two 3D segments added to a 3D axes reports `len(get_segments()) == 0` before `fig.canvas.draw()` and two segments of shape (2, 2), the projection, after it; its 3D end points are held only in the private `_segments3d`. `(Path(d) / "replay\x00.css").resolve()` raises `ValueError` ("embedded null character in path"), so an `href` resolved without the NUL guard stops the check instead of failing it; `"//[bad"` is the URL `urlsplit` refuses in pull request 2's test of malformed image URLs.
- **Q18. Round 5's probes**, at `6b14c26`, Matplotlib 3.10.8. `ax.fill_between([0.0], [1.0], [2.0])` makes one path of 5 vertices; with empty arrays, none. Under `style(theme)`, in both themes, a 3D axes' three `pane`s, `line`s and `gridlines` are not among `fig.findobj()`, and `visible_colours` of the figure is the same set before and after `set_pane_color(background)` on all three; the default panes are `#f2f2f2`, `#e6e6e6` and `#ececec` with alpha 0.5 (face and edge, edge width 1), off the palette and off the background-to-ink segment in both themes, while after a draw the axis lines and gridlines are palette colours (`#d5c4a1` and `#ebe2bd` light, `#504945` and `#2b2f30` dark). A `Line3D` made from (1, 2), (3, 4), (5, 6) and then given one point with `set_data_3d` still reports `get_xdata()` `[1, 2]` until a draw, and its one projected x after it.
- **Q20. Task 1's label rules, measured with the probe's code at `d2aa745`.** `clip_probe.label_masses` on `record(n_observations=9, n_particles=40)`, light theme: `demo_multi_mass` places mass 1 and mass 2 at `(9, 7)` and `(9, 7)` points; `demo_multi_mass_2d` at `(-9, 7)` and `(9, 7)`. The small model-comparison replay's posterior is `(1/3, 1/3, 1/3)` at row 0 and `(0, 0.5829, 0.4171)` and `(0, 0.9108, 0.0892)` at rows 4 and 9; on the probe's panel (rectangle height 0.74 of 4.2 inches, y-limits −0.03 to 1.03) the heads at rows 1 to 9 are at least 12.9 points apart (12.928, the closest pair, at row 3), and the lowest head, K = 1's at 0, is 6.3 points above the panel's bottom edge, so the rule of D50 moves no label there, stacks the three at row 0 (shifts 0, −12, −24 points), and never needs the rise at the bottom. These heights are the probe's layout; Task 5's own layout may move them a little, which `test_model_labels_follow_the_heads` computes rather than assumes.
- **Q19. Round 6's probe**, at `c05ef00`. The committed echolocation archive's whole prior row, 500 dots of which 498 lie outside `view_bounds`, drawn uncropped as a marker-only `Line3D` (marker size 1, alpha 0.5) in a 3D axes with those limits, at azimuth −75, and then hidden: 2,454 pixels differ inside the axes' window extent and none outside it. The dots outside the cube are clipped to the axes, so the differential test of the close view cannot see a missing crop.

Not measured, and where each is settled: Quarto 1.11.9, the Linux encoder binary and runner timings (Task 1); the real clips' draw times and sizes (Task 1; the design's M9 has 2.1 s per theme for the 2D prototype and 12.5 s for echolocation); Chrome, Firefox and Safari playback (Task 13).

### Task 1: Draw and time every clip; build on Quarto 1.11.9; time the runner; settle the format

Nothing here is library code. The task finds out, before any test pins a layout, whether the six clips can be drawn from the archives as they are, how long they take, and whether Quarto 1.11.9 and the Linux encoder behave as the design measured on Quarto 1.9.32 and macOS.

**Files:**
- Create: `docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py`; seven sheets `docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip-<demo>.jpg` (six) and `clip-demo_2d-at-0.10.jpg`
- Modify: `docs/plans/2026-11-24-replay-clips.md` (a "Probe results" section, added at the end)
- On a throwaway branch only, never on `claude-replay-clips`: `.github/workflows/clip-probe.yml`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `load_replay` from `clocks._replay`; `style`, `THEMES` from `clocks._style`; `trace_limits`, `trace_header` from `clocks._figures`; the six committed archives; `clocks._demos.demo_2d`, to record the 2D run at 0.10 in memory.
- Produces, in `clip_probe.py`, three module-level values that Tasks 3 to 6 copy into the library:
  - `LAYOUTS: dict[str, dict[str, tuple[float, float, float, float]]]`, for each of the six demos its axes gids (the vocabulary of Global Constraints) mapped to rectangles in figure fractions (an inset's rectangle is in fractions of its parent axes);
  - `SIDE_OFFSET: float`, the distance in x between a clock's three marks in the slowing panel;
  - `GUTTER: float`, the width, in observations, of the model-comparison clip's label gutter.
  The script takes one of `--sheets DIR`, `--time DIR`, `--decode DIR`, `--quarto DIR`; it holds no absolute path and passes `ruff format --check` and `ruff check`. Whatever it prints about the machine (working directory, `sys.prefix`, executable paths) goes to standard output for the run's log and is never written into a committed file.

- [x] **Step 1: Write the probe's drawing.** One throwaway function per demo, `draw_<demo>(replay, colors, row, azimuth) -> Figure`, following section 5 of the design and the artist paragraphs of Tasks 3 to 6 below, on a figure of 7.5 inches by the demo's declared height made with `plt.figure(figsize=...)` followed by `fig.set_layout_engine("none")` (Q3). Rectangles come from `LAYOUTS`; start from the design's arrangement (cloud or map at left, traces at right; for the two track clips the clock strip and the slowing panel under the cloud; for echolocation the whole-prior box at upper left, the scene at left, traces stacked at right and the fit panel as a small square).

- [x] **Step 2: Draw the sheets and look at them.** `uv run python docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py --sheets docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation` writes one JPEG per demo: rows k = 0, 1, 8 and the last, in both themes, each at 1500 pixels wide and resampled to 780 (390 CSS pixels at twice density), composed with Pillow and saved with `image.save(path, quality=85)`, no `exif` and no `comment`. **The 2D clip is drawn twice**: from the committed archive (M = 0.15) and from `demo_2d.record()` called with `demo_2d.TRUE_M` set to 0.10 in the probe's own process (nothing is written to `assets/`), because Task 11 changes that run and its inset window, trace limits and clock fills with it (Q6). For each sheet, report each item as seen or not seen, and change `LAYOUTS`, `SIDE_OFFSET` or `GUTTER` and redraw until all are seen: no text overlaps text or a data mark at full width; the status line and every trace header can be read at 780 pixels, the six headers of the two-mass 2D clip included; no inset covers its truth or the final cloud, in either 2D sheet; the three marks of each clock in the slowing panel stand apart; the prior's prediction bars are visible at k = 0 without flattening the later bars beyond reading; `belief` and `belief_alt` clouds can be told apart by marker in both themes; the echolocation ring is open and the final cloud is visible inside it; the whole-prior box is full at k = 0 and holds one cluster at k = 1; the model-comparison labels never stack; every figure-level rectangle lies inside the unit square and no two overlap (Task 3's `test_panels_are_on_the_canvas_and_apart`), the echolocation whole-prior box beside the scene and not over it; the track clips' strip and slowing panels have exactly the cloud's left edge and width (Task 4's `test_track_panels_share_the_x_scale`); nothing in any frame is drawn in a colour outside the palette (the 3D panes and grid included).

- [x] **Step 3: Time every clip, and decode the small clips, here.** `uv run --with imageio-ffmpeg==0.8.1 python docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py --time <a directory outside the repository>` renders each of the six clips in both themes, every frame of the dwell schedule, through the encoder with the settings of Global Constraints, decodes each, and prints one line per clip (demo, theme, drawings, seconds to draw and encode, seconds to decode, bytes, decoded frame count) and one total. Expected frame counts: 296 for `demo_1d` and `demo_2d`, 356 for the other four. `--decode <directory>` repeats Q2's small-clip measurements with that encoder and prints, for each, the exit status, the first `Video:` line and the progress report's `frame=` and `progress=` lines: three solid frames of 64 by 48 written 2, 1 and 4 times; that clip re-encoded with `-c:v mpeg4`, and with `-c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv444p`; made again at 25 frames a second, and at 64 by 40; copied with `-c copy` and no `+faststart`; sixty frames of seeded noise cut to half its bytes; and a text file named `.mp4`. It exits non-zero unless the whole clips report 7 frames, the stream properties each was made with and `progress=end`; the cut clip reports either a non-zero exit status or a frame count from 1 to 59; and the text file reports a non-zero exit status.

- [x] **Step 4: Build a scratch Quarto project.** `clip_probe.py --quarto DIR` writes, into an empty directory outside the repository, a project with the site's `custom.scss`, `custom-dark.scss` and `styles.css` (copied from `site/`), a `replay.css` holding the rule of decision D45 and a final newline, a `_quarto.yml` whose `project` has `type: website`, `output-dir: _output`, `pre-render: prerender.py` and `resources: ["media/*.mp4", "media/*.png"]` and whose HTML format has the site's `theme` (`light: [custom.scss]`, `dark: [custom-dark.scss]`) and `css: [styles.css, replay.css]`, a `prerender.py` that writes a twenty-frame clip and a poster per theme into `media/` and appends its working directory, `sys.prefix`, `sys.version` and `QUARTO_PROJECT_RENDER_ALL` to a log file in that scratch directory, and two pages, `index.qmd` and `story/page.qmd`, each holding the block of decision D22 for `demo_2d` and a `::: {#demo-2d-text}` div. It then runs `quarto render` itself, as a child process of the probe that inherits its environment, never through another `uv run` (which would drop the `--with imageio-ffmpeg` overlay and give the pre-render script another interpreter): a first render, with the output directory removed beforehand; a second `quarto render` with two sentinels planted, `output-only.mp4` in `_output/media/` alone and `source-only.mp4` in `media/` alone (one file in both places could not tell a deletion from a fresh copy), `quarto render story/page.qmd`, and a last render with a pre-render script that exits 1, and exits non-zero unless all of these hold:
  - (a) the pre-render script ran in the project directory, under the interpreter the command was started with, and saw `QUARTO_PROJECT_RENDER_ALL=1` on the full renders and no such value on the single-page render;
  - (b) after the clean full render, `_output/media/` holds the four files;
  - (c) in each rendered page, the two `<video ...></video>` lines, the `<figure class="replay">` line and the `<figcaption>` line of the source block appear unchanged, attribute for attribute;
  - (d) each page has exactly one element with `id="demo-2d-text"`, holding the div's text;
  - (e) the page's `<body>` has the class `quarto-light`, and a stylesheet under `_output/site_libs/` holds a rule hiding `.light-content` under `body.quarto-dark` and one hiding `.dark-content` under `body.quarto-light`;
  - (f) `_output/replay.css` exists and is byte for byte the source file, and each of the two rendered pages, the root one and the one in a subdirectory, has a `<link rel="stylesheet">` whose `href`, resolved from the page, is that file (if Quarto bundles, renames or rewrites a `css:` file, decision D45's form cannot work as it stands: a stop condition);
  - (g) the render whose pre-render script exits 1 exits non-zero;
  - (h) after the second full render `_output/media/output-only.mp4` is gone: Quarto clears its output directory on a full render. (`source-only.mp4` is then in `_output/media/`, copied as a resource, since the scratch script removes nothing; that is recorded, and is what the real step's cleanup is for.) The reviewer saw both on Quarto 1.9.32.
  Run it with the local Quarto and record its version.

- [x] **Step 5: Run Steps 3 and 4 on a cold runner with Quarto 1.11.9.** Commit the probe script and the sheets to `claude-replay-clips` and push, after the full local gate of Global Constraints (`ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, the default suite and the slow suite: the script is linted, and nothing else may have moved) and the sheets' metadata check of Step 6. Then, from the worktree, `git worktree add ../claude-replay-clips-probe -b claude-replay-clips-probe HEAD`; in that second worktree add `.github/workflows/clip-probe.yml` as below, commit and `git push -u origin claude-replay-clips-probe`. The workflow file never enters `claude-replay-clips`: `tests/test_generated_assets.py::test_the_ci_bullets_link_exactly_the_workflow_files` requires the Reproducibility page to link exactly the files under `.github/workflows/`. **The disposable branch is the one stated exception to the gate**: with a fifth workflow file that test fails there by construction, the branch runs only the probe workflow, is never reviewed or merged, and is deleted in this step.

```yaml
name: Clip probe (temporary)

on:
  push:
    branches: [claude-replay-clips-probe]

jobs:
  probe:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    timeout-minutes: 15
    permissions:
      contents: read
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
      - uses: quarto-dev/quarto-actions/setup@v2
        with:
          version: "1.11.9"
      - uses: astral-sh/setup-uv@v8.2.0
        with:
          version: "latest"
      - name: Set up Python 3.12
        run: uv python install 3.12
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: uv sync --frozen
      - name: Say which interpreter the site jobs get
        run: uv run --frozen python -c "import sys; print(sys.version)"
      - name: Decode the small clips
        run: uv run --frozen --with imageio-ffmpeg==0.8.1 python docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py --decode "$RUNNER_TEMP/decode"
      - name: Time every clip
        run: uv run --frozen --with imageio-ffmpeg==0.8.1 python docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py --time "$RUNNER_TEMP/clips"
      - name: Quarto 1.11.9 clean build
        run: uv run --frozen --with imageio-ffmpeg==0.8.1 python docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py --quarto "$RUNNER_TEMP/quarto-probe"
```

  The setup steps are those of `site-check.yml` and `site.yml`, unchanged, so the probe measures the environment the site jobs will have; they install Python 3.12 without selecting it, and the extra step logs which interpreter `uv run --frozen` then uses (decision D42). Find the run by branch and commit, which does not depend on a workflow file that `main` lacks: `gh run list --branch claude-replay-clips-probe --commit <the full 40-character SHA of the probe commit> --json databaseId,headSha,status,conclusion`; wait for a non-empty `conclusion`, and read the steps' output with `gh run view <id> --log`. Then `git push origin --delete claude-replay-clips-probe`, `git worktree remove ../claude-replay-clips-probe` and `git branch -D claude-replay-clips-probe`. The run's log stays reachable by its URL.

- [x] **Step 6: Record the results in this plan, whatever they are.** Add a section "Probe results" at the end of this plan, with: the local and runner Quarto versions and items (a) to (h) for each; the interpreter and the encoder's version line on the runner; the decode probe's lines, here and on the runner; per clip and theme, drawings, seconds and bytes, here and on the runner, the two totals, and the probe job's whole duration; the final `LAYOUTS`, `SIDE_OFFSET` and `GUTTER`, by reference to the script; every change made to decisions D17, D18 and D20; and the run's URL. It is written before Step 7's decision and committed in Step 8 whichever way that decision goes. The same numbers go to the coordinator for the pull request's description. Before staging any sheet, check its metadata as pull request 2 did: `Image.open(path).getexif()` is empty, `set(Image.open(path).info) == {"jfif", "jfif_density", "jfif_unit", "jfif_version"}`, and `Image.open(path).applist` is one `APP0` segment whose data start `b"JFIF\x00"`; and look at the pixels, the staged text and the commit body for a path or a machine's name.

- [x] **Step 7: Decide whether to go on.** Stop, change nothing further, and report to the coordinator if any of these holds:
  - **The format.** A panel of section 5 cannot be drawn from the keys `validate_replay` accepts without importing `clocks.physics` or `clocks.inference` or running a demo again; or a sheet shows that a panel misleads for want of data (for example, the 500 dots cannot show the two-mass 2D cloud's second mode, or an inset shows float32 steps). Planning found every panel's inputs present: the slowing panel reads `predicted_mean`, `predicted_std`, `observations`, `true_rates` and `noise_std`; the 2D clock fills read `observations`; the model lines read `model_posterior`, `model_ks` and `true_k`; the echolocation panels read `view_bounds`, `weight_in_view`, `predicted_mean` and the centred `observations`. If the stop condition holds all the same, the design's rule applies: the format version is raised and the archives re-recorded in this pull request. **That is a plan amendment made by the coordinator and reviewed before any later task starts**; nothing of it is done on the implementer's own judgement. The amendment, a Task 1b, must settle each of these, and this plan settles none of them in advance:
    - what the loader does with a version-1 file: refuse it (the design's "the format version is raised" read strictly, no reader of old files being needed since all seven are re-recorded) or read both; and, either way, a test that loads a version-1 literal fixture and shows the decided behaviour;
    - the version tests: `tests/test_replay.py::test_wrong_format_or_version_is_rejected` uses `version=2` as its invalid case today, so with `VERSION = 2` it must take a version that is invalid then (0, 3, a string) and `test_module_constants` the new value;
    - the new keys in `_array_specs`, in the design's section 1 table and in the `record()` of each demo that needs them, with the literal fixtures of `tests/test_replay.py`;
