  - **Time.** With `t` the probe's total for the twelve clips on the runner, drawing, encoding and decoding together: at most 120 s, go on; from 120 s to 240 s, go on only if the latest `Site check` run's duration plus `t` is under eight of the job's ten minutes (`gh run list --workflow site-check.yml --limit 1 --json databaseId` and that run's timing), and say so in "Probe results"; above 240 s, or failing that sum, stop. The design expects under two minutes; the site jobs render in 43 to 61 s today (design, M13).
  - **Quarto.** Any of (a) to (h) fails on 1.11.9. (Item (c), which stopped the probe, is superseded by decision D22: the rendered markup is compared as Quarto writes it.) Item (h) in particular is not worked around in a later task: if a full render leaves an output-only file in place, the supported build command would no longer establish the published set, and how to restore that is a decision for the coordinator.
  - **The encoder.** `imageio-ffmpeg`'s Linux binary does not run on the runner, has no `libx264`, or fails the decode probe.
  - **The slowing panel.** The prior's prediction bars cannot be kept in view without flattening the later bars beyond reading. The design says the prior's bars are shown; leaving them out of the limits would be a change to the design, made there first (decision D19).

  A layout that fails any other item of Step 2's list is not a stop condition: change the layout values, redraw, and say what moved.

- [x] **Step 8: Run the full local gate, read its output, and then, in a separate command, commit and push** the plan's "Probe results", whether the decision was to go on or to stop (the script and sheets were pushed in Step 5), as `Record the clip probe: sheets, timings, Quarto 1.11.9 and the runner`.

### Task 2: `_video.py`: the dwell schedule, the encoder, the decode check

**Files:**
- Create: `src/clocks/_video.py`, `tests/test_video.py`
- Modify: `src/clocks/_style.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`
- Test: `tests/test_video.py`, `tests/test_style.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_video.py`, which imports only the standard library at module level:

```python
FPS = 20

class EncoderNotFound(RuntimeError): ...
class EncoderError(RuntimeError): ...

class StreamInfo(NamedTuple):
    codec: str        # "h264"
    pix_fmt: str      # "yuv420p"
    width: int
    height: int
    fps: float
    frames: int       # frames the encoder decoded, not a count read from the container
    moov_first: bool  # the moov box comes before mdat

def dwell_frames(n_observations: int) -> list[int]: ...
def find_encoder() -> str: ...
def encoder_version(encoder: str) -> str: ...
def encode(frames: Iterable[tuple[bytes, int]], path: Path, *, width: int, height: int,
           encoder: str | None = None) -> int: ...
def parse_stream_line(text: str) -> tuple[str, str, int, int, float]: ...
def parse_progress(text: str) -> int: ...
def box_order(path: Path) -> list[str]: ...
def probe(path: Path, encoder: str | None = None) -> StreamInfo: ...

def _run_encoder(command: Sequence[str], frames: Iterable[bytes]) -> tuple[int, str]: ...
def _run_decoder(command: Sequence[str]) -> tuple[int, str, str]: ...
```

  - `dwell_frames(T)` returns T + 1 counts, one per row: 24 for row 0; for 0 < k < T, 18 when k ≤ 3, 10 when k ≤ 8, 5 when k ≤ 20, else 2; 50 for row T. `ValueError` for T < 1.
  - `find_encoder()` returns `imageio_ffmpeg.get_ffmpeg_exe()` when that import and call succeed (an `ImportError`, or the `RuntimeError` the package raises when it has no binary, means "not this way"); else `shutil.which("ffmpeg")`; else raises `EncoderNotFound("no FFmpeg encoder found: install imageio-ffmpeg (in a checkout, `uv sync` provides it) or put ffmpeg on PATH")`.
  - `_run_encoder(command, frames)` is the one place a process is started for encoding: it starts `command` with a pipe for standard input and a temporary file for standard error, writes each frame, closes the pipe, waits, and returns the exit status and the standard error's text. A `BrokenPipeError` raised by a write to the pipe, or by closing it, means the encoder's input pipe can no longer accept writes (on the builds measured, because the encoder has exited): writing stops, a second `BrokenPipeError` from the close that follows is ignored, and the status and text are returned as usual. Any exception raised by the `frames` iterable itself, a `BrokenPipeError` of its own included, is the caller's: it ends the encoder (`terminate`) and propagates. **On every path the pipe is closed or the process terminated first, and the process is then waited for, before the function returns or raises**, so none is left unreaped and none is waited for while it still waits for input. Standard error goes to a file, not a pipe, so a failing encoder that prints a great deal cannot block the frame writes (this is not tested: a blocked pipe would hang the suite, not fail it). `_run_decoder(command)` runs `command` to its end and returns the exit status, standard output and standard error. Tests replace these two functions to assert arguments and failure handling without a shell script, and drive the real encoder for everything about the stream.
  - `encode` pipes each frame, `width * height * 4` bytes of RGBA, to the encoder as many times as its count, and returns the total written. It streams: the iterable it hands `_run_encoder` takes one `(frame, count)` pair at a time from the caller's iterable, checks that pair's length, and yields the frame `count` times, so nothing is buffered (a 356-frame echolocation clip is about 2.3 GB of raw frames) and a wrong length is found only when its pair is reached, after earlier frames have been written. The message is `f"frame {n} has {len(frame)} bytes, expected {width * height * 4}"`, with `n` the pair's 1-based position in the caller's iterable, not a count of video frames. It looks for the encoder (when none is passed) before asking the iterable for anything. What it writes: a temporary file beside `path`, named by appending to its name, `f"{path.name}.{token}.part"` with a random `token` (the directory must exist), moved over `path` with `os.replace` only after the encoder has exited with status 0. On any failure the temporary file is removed, `path` is left exactly as it was (an older file stays, a missing one stays missing), and the error is `ValueError` for an odd `width` or `height` (raised before the encoder starts) or a frame of the wrong length, the caller's own exception if the iterable raised, and otherwise `EncoderError` naming the encoder, its exit status, `path` and the last lines of its standard error. The command is exactly: `[encoder, "-hide_banner", "-loglevel", "error", "-nostdin", "-y", "-f", "rawvideo", "-pix_fmt", "rgba", "-s", f"{width}x{height}", "-r", str(FPS), "-i", "-", "-an", "-c:v", "libx264", "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p", "-crf", "20", "-preset", "slow", "-movflags", "+faststart", "-f", "mp4", str(temporary)]`.
  - `probe` runs `[encoder, "-hide_banner", "-nostdin", "-i", str(path), "-map", "0:v:0", "-f", "null", "-", "-progress", "pipe:1", "-nostats"]` through `_run_decoder` and distinguishes three outcomes. **The decoder failed**: a non-zero exit status raises `ValueError(f"{path}: the decoder failed (exit status {status}): {last lines of standard error}")`, whatever else it printed. **Its report cannot be read**: `parse_stream_line` or `parse_progress` raises `ValueError`, which `probe` re-raises with the path in front. **It decoded**: a `StreamInfo` with the stream line's properties, the progress report's frame count and `moov_first` from `box_order`. A file cut short is, on the builds measured, the third outcome with fewer frames (Q2, Q13); callers compare the count.
  - `parse_stream_line(text)` finds the first line containing `Video:` and returns codec, pixel format, width, height and frames per second from it: the word after `Video:`, the word after the first comma that follows, the first `<digits>x<digits>` after that, and the number before ` fps`. `ValueError` quoting the line when any part is missing, and `ValueError("no video stream ...")` when no line has `Video:`.
  - `parse_progress(text)` reads the `key=value` lines `-progress` writes. They come in records, each ended by a `progress=` line. The last non-empty line must be `progress=end`, and the result is the `frame=` value of **that last record**: the lines after the previous `progress=` line, or from the start if there is none. `ValueError("the decoder's progress report did not finish ...")` when the last line is anything else (`progress=continue`, or nothing), and `ValueError("... no frame count ...")` when the last record has no `frame=` line or its value is not a non-negative integer; a count in an earlier record does not stand in for one.
  - `box_order(path)` returns the four-character types of the file's top-level boxes in order, following each box's 32-bit size (a size of 1 means a 64-bit size follows the type; a size of 0, a size smaller than the box's own header, 8 bytes or 16 with a 64-bit size, or one that runs past the end of the file, ends the walk after that box is listed, so every step advances and the walk ends).
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_style.py`: `frame_rgba(fig: Figure) -> bytes`, the pixels `save_png` writes, as rows of RGBA with no header. `save_png` and `frame_rgba` call one private function with `save_png`'s present settings, differing only in `format`.
- `pyproject.toml`: `imageio-ffmpeg>=0.8.1` in the `dev` group only (`uv add --dev "imageio-ffmpeg>=0.8.1"`, which also rewrites `uv.lock`).

### Task 3: The clip parts, the `demo_2d` clip, and `render_clip`

**Files:**
- Create: `src/clocks/_clip_parts.py`, `src/clocks/_clip_maps.py`, `src/clocks/_clips.py`, `tests/_clip_replays.py`, `tests/test_clips.py`
- Modify: `src/clocks/viz.py`, `tests/_figure_artists.py`, `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`, `scripts/check_site_links.py`, `tests/test_site_prose_contract.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `Replay`, `DEMOS` from `clocks._replay`; `Palette`, `style`, `frame_rgba` from `clocks._style`; `trace_limits`, `trace_header` from `clocks._figures`; `dwell_frames`, `find_encoder`, `encode` from `clocks._video`; `LAYOUTS["demo_2d"]` from Task 1's probe script.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_clip_parts.py`:

```python
Rect = tuple[float, float, float, float]  # left, bottom, width, height

class Clip:
    """A figure with fixed axes, and the function that sets its artists to one row."""
    def __init__(self, fig: Figure, update: Callable[[int, float | None], None]) -> None: ...
    fig: Figure
    def show(self, row: int, azimuth: float | None = None) -> None: ...

def new_figure(height: int) -> Figure: ...
def status_text(row: int) -> str: ...
def draw_status(fig: Figure, colors: Palette) -> Callable[[int], None]: ...
def standard_deviations(replay: Replay) -> NDArray[np.float64]: ...
def draw_traces(fig: Figure, replay: Replay, colors: Palette,
                rects: Mapping[str, Rect]) -> Callable[[int], None]: ...
def draw_dots(ax: Axes, color: str, *, gid: str = "dots", marker: str = "o") -> Line2D: ...
def ellipse_points(mean: NDArray, cov: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.float64]: ...
def draw_ellipse(ax: Axes, color: str, *, gid: str = "ellipse") -> Line2D: ...
def draw_truth(ax: Axes, x: float, y: float, color: str, *, gid: str = "truth") -> Line2D: ...
def zoom_window(truth: NDArray, mean: NDArray, sd: NDArray,
                prior_ranges: Sequence[Sequence[float]]) -> NDArray[np.float64]: ...
def zoom_label(x_factor: float, y_factor: float, y_name: str) -> str: ...
def draw_inset(ax: Axes, rect: Rect, window: NDArray, colors: Palette, *,
               gid: str = "inset") -> Axes: ...
def running_slowing(observations: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.float64]: ...
```

  - `new_figure(height)` returns `plt.figure(figsize=(7.5, height / 100))` after `fig.set_layout_engine("none")` (Q3; `layout="none"` at creation is not enough).
  - `status_text(0) == "Before any observation"`, `status_text(1) == "After 1 observation"`, `status_text(k) == f"After {k} observations"`. `draw_status` adds a figure-level text with gid `"status"`, colour `ink`, anchored top left, and returns the function that sets it for a row.
  - `standard_deviations(replay)` is the square root of `cov`'s diagonal, shape (T + 1, P).
  - `draw_traces` adds one axes per key of `rects`, each key `"trace-<name>"` for a name in `meta["param_names"]`, with that gid. In each: a `Line2D` `"trace-mean"` (`belief`); a band `"trace-band"` (`belief`, face alpha above 0) from mean − sd to mean + sd; a `Line2D` `"trace-truth"` (`truth`, solid) from `[0, T]` at the truth; x-limits (0, T); y-limits `trace_limits(mean column, sd column, truth, prior range)`. The returned function, for a row k, gives the mean line the points 0..k, redraws the band over 0..k (remove it and add it again: Matplotlib 3.9, the oldest the package declares, cannot reset a band's data), hides the band at row 0 and shows it from row 1 (at row 0 it is one point wide, and `fill_between` still makes a path of it: Q18), and sets the title to `trace_header(name, truth, mean[k], sd[k], prior range)`.
  - `draw_dots` returns a marker-only `Line2D` (`linestyle="none"`, the marker given, a marker size above 0, an alpha strictly between 0 and 1) with no data yet.
  - `ellipse_points(mean, cov)` takes a 2-vector and a 2 by 2 block and returns 121 points, the last equal to the first: `mean + 2 * (V * sqrt(clip(w, 0, None))) @ [cos t, sin t]` for `w, V = np.linalg.eigh(cov)` and t evenly spaced over a full turn. A zero block gives 121 copies of `mean`.
  - `draw_truth` returns a marker-only `Line2D` at (x, y): an open ring (`marker="o"`, `markerfacecolor="none"`, an edge in the colour given with a width above 0).
  - `zoom_window(truth, mean, sd, prior_ranges)` takes one entry per axis and returns per axis `(truth - h, truth + h)` with `h = 1.25 * (abs(mean - truth) + 2 * sd)`, or `0.01 * (high - low)` of that axis's prior range where `h` would be 0.
  - `zoom_label(x_factor, y_factor, y_name)`: `f"zoom ×{round(x_factor)}"` when the two round to the same integer, else `f"zoom ×{round(x_factor)} (x), ×{round(y_factor)} ({y_name})"`.
  - `draw_inset` adds a child axes of `ax` at `rect` (in fractions of `ax`) with the given gid and the window as its limits, no tick labels, spines in `muted`.
  - `running_slowing(observations)` returns shape (T + 1, C): row k is the mean of `1 - observations[:k]` over its first axis; row 0 is NaN.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_clip_maps.py`: `LAYOUT_2D: dict[str, Rect]` with the keys `"map"`, `"inset"`, `"trace-x"`, `"trace-y"`, `"trace-M"`; `clock_fill(running: NDArray, largest: float, colors: Palette) -> list[tuple[float, float, float, float]]`; `build_2d(replay: Replay, colors: Palette) -> Clip`. `clock_fill` returns one RGBA per clock. Where the value is NaN (the prior row): `(0, 0, 0, 0)`, a hollow clock. Otherwise `background + t * (ink - background)` with alpha 1, where `t = min(max(value / largest, 0), 1)` when `largest > 0`, and `t = 0` for every clock when `largest <= 0`: if no clock was ever slow on average, none is filled, and nothing is divided by zero or by a negative number.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_clips.py`:

```python
CLIP_HEIGHTS: dict[str, int] = {
    "demo_1d": 450, "demo_2d": 450, "demo_multi_mass": 500,
    "demo_multi_mass_2d": 550, "demo_model_comparison": 420,
    "demo_echolocation_3d": 550,
}
BUILDERS: dict[str, Callable[[Replay, Palette], Clip]]  # {"demo_2d": build_2d} after this task

class Shot(NamedTuple):
    row: int
    repeat: int
    azimuth: float | None = None

def shots(replay: Replay) -> list[Shot]: ...
def build_clip(replay: Replay, colors: Palette) -> Clip: ...
def final_frame(replay: Replay, colors: Palette) -> Figure: ...
def render_clip(replay: Replay, path: Path, *, theme: str = "light",
                encoder: str | None = None) -> int: ...
```

  - `shots(replay)` is `[Shot(k, n) for k, n in enumerate(dwell_frames(T))]` (Task 6 adds the camera for one demo).
  - `build_clip` raises `ValueError(f"no clip for demo {demo!r}")` for a demo with no builder. `final_frame` builds the clip, shows the last shot and returns the figure; the caller saves and closes it. Both must be called inside `with style(theme) as colors:`.
  - `render_clip` finds the encoder first (when none is passed); then, inside `with style(theme) as colors:`, builds the clip and gives `encode` one `(frame_rgba(clip.fig), shot.repeat)` per shot, each taken after `clip.show(shot.row, shot.azimuth)`, with `width=1500` and `height=2 * CLIP_HEIGHTS[demo]`; closes the figure whether or not the encode succeeded; returns `encode`'s count. It writes what `encode` writes and nothing else.
- `clocks.viz` gains `render_clip` in its imports and `__all__`, and its docstring names `clocks._clips`.
- `scripts/check_site_links.py`: one of `FORBIDDEN_CURRENT_CLAIMS`, `"_" + "cli"` (the old command module), is matched only where no word character follows it, by the regex `_cli(?!\w)` on the case-folded text, instead of as a substring; every other claim is matched as today. Without this, every current file that names `clocks._clips` or a `_clip_*` module (from this task on: `viz.py`, `_clips.py`, `_clip_maps.py`, and later `_site_media.py`) trips the claim, and `tests/test_site_prose_contract.py::test_repository_current_prose_contract_is_clean` and the link checker fail at every gate. The new set of word-ended claims is spelled as the dictionary spells its key, `"_" + "cli"`, so the script does not trip itself.
- Produces, in `tests/_figure_artists.py`: `all_axes(fig) -> list[Axes]` (each of `fig.axes` followed by its `child_axes`); `off_palette(fig, colors) -> set[str]` (the colours that are neither in `palette_hexes(colors)` nor within 1.5/255 per channel of a point on the segment from `background` to `ink`, among `visible_colours(fig)` and, for every 3D axes of `all_axes(fig)`, the face of each of its three axes' `pane`, the pane's edge when its line width is above 0, the colour of each axis's `line` and the colours of its `gridlines`, read as `visible_colours` reads colours; `fig.findobj()` reaches none of these, so `visible_colours` alone never sees a default grey pane (measured, Q18); the figure must have been drawn first, since a 3D axes sets its grid colours at draw time); `opaque_fills_above(ax, artist) -> list[Artist]` (visible artists drawn directly on `ax` after `artist`, that is with a higher `zorder`, or an equal one and a later place in `ax.get_children()`, since Matplotlib draws equal `zorder` in the order artists were added, that paint an opaque fill: a patch or collection whose face has alpha 1, or a line whose marker can be filled (`MarkerStyle(marker).is_filled()`) and whose marker face, not `"none"`, has alpha 1 with the line's own alpha `None` or 1; the axes' own background patch and its child axes are not counted: an inset covers the part of its panel it sits on by design, and Task 1's sheets check that it covers neither the truth nor the final cloud; it is for 2D axes only, where Matplotlib draws in `zorder` order, and it is coarse on purpose, not asking whether the artist it finds overlaps anything: decision D46); and `assert_shown(artist, kind)` and `assert_hidden(artist)`. `assert_shown` asserts, on the artist itself and whatever its axes, that it puts ink on the canvas: it and every ancestor up to the figure are visible; its alpha is `None` or above 0; and, by `kind`, that it has something to draw and a stroke or a fill to draw it with. An *effective stroke* is a width above 0 together with a colour whose alpha is above 0; an *effective fill* is a face colour, other than `"none"`, whose alpha is above 0. `"marks"` (a marker-only line): at least one data point (for a 3D line, counted from `get_data_3d()`: a `Line3D`'s `get_xdata()` keeps whatever it last held until the next draw, measured in Q18), a marker other than none, a marker size above 0, and either an effective stroke of the marker's edge (`markeredgewidth` and the edge colour) or, **only for a marker that can be filled** (`MarkerStyle(marker).is_filled()`: true for `"o"`, `"s"`, `"^"`, false for `"_"`; Q16), an effective fill of its face. So a hollow ring with no edge width fails, and so does a `"_"` tick with no edge width, whatever its face colour. `"line"`: at least two points (from `get_data_3d()` for a 3D line, as for marks), a line style other than none, and an effective stroke. `"band"`: at least one path of three or more vertices and an effective fill. `"segments"` (a line collection): some one segment i whose two end points differ and whose own stroke is effective, its line width and edge colour being entry i of `get_linewidths()` and `get_edgecolors()`, each taken modulo its length as Matplotlib cycles them; so a wireframe with a line width and transparent edges fails, and so does one whose only visible segment has no length. The segments are read with `get_segments()` after the figure has been drawn: a `Line3DCollection` holds its projected 2D segments only after a draw, and none before it (measured, Q17); Matplotlib has no public getter for its 3D end points. `"scatter"` (a collection of markers): some one item i with an offset, a size above 0 (entry i of `get_sizes()`, modulo its length) and an effective stroke or an effective fill of its own (entry i of the edge colours with the line widths, or of the face colours, likewise). Geometry, size and colour must belong to the same item: checked across separate items, a transparent drawable item beside an opaque one of no size or length would pass. `"text"`: a non-empty string whose colour's alpha is above 0. The helper's claim is drawable geometry and styling; whether the ink lands where a reader can see it is the contact sheets' and, for the 3D cloud, the differential render's (decision D46). An entry that shows only `"after the prior"` may, at row 0, be hidden or have nothing to draw (no point, one point for a line, no path). `assert_hidden` asserts that the artist or one of its ancestors is not visible. The existing `one()` looks only at the visibility of the owning axes, and `assert_marks_drawn` only at the clouds in `DOTS`.
- Produces, in `tests/_clip_replays.py` (a helper module, not collected), each result cached per name: `small(name) -> Replay`, `importlib.import_module(f"clocks._demos.{name}").record(n_observations=9, n_particles=40)`; `shortest(name)`, `record(n_observations=1, n_particles=16)`; `one_particle(name)`, `record(n_observations=2, n_particles=1)`; `committed(name)`, `load_replay` of the committed archive; `edited(replay, **arrays) -> Replay`, a copy with those arrays replaced; and the tables the shared tests read, which Tasks 4 to 6 extend:

```python
# demo -> (axes gid, artist gid, (x column, y column) of cloud, palette role)
DOTS = {"demo_2d": [("map", "dots", (0, 1), "belief")]}
# demo -> (axes gid, artist gid, (i, j): the block of mean and cov, palette role)
ELLIPSES = {"demo_2d": [("map", "ellipse", (0, 1), "belief")]}
# demo -> (axes gid, artist gid, (i, j): the entries of true_params)
TRUTHS = {"demo_2d": [("map", "truth", (0, 1))]}
# demo -> (parent axes gid, inset gid, (i, j), y name, the parent artists it repeats);
# each copy's gid is f"{inset_gid}-{artist_gid}", both taken from the row
INSETS = {"demo_2d": [("map", "inset", (0, 1), "y", ("dots", "truth", "ellipse"))]}
```

**What must be drawn.** A value assertion passes on an artist that is hidden, transparent or has no marker, so every clip also lists the artists a reader must see. `tests/_clip_replays.py` holds `ARTISTS: dict[str, list[tuple[str | None, str, str, str]]]`, each entry an axes gid (`None` for a figure-level text), an artist gid, a `kind` of `assert_shown`, and when it shows: `"always"`, `"after the prior"` (hidden, or without a second point, at row 0) or `"only the prior"` (Task 6 adds a fourth for one entry, `"when any is in view"`). This task's entries, for `"demo_2d"`: in `"map"`, `("dots", "marks", "always")`, `("clocks", "scatter", "always")`, `("truth", "marks", "always")`, `("ellipse", "line", "always")`; in `"inset"`, `"inset-dots"`, `"inset-truth"` and `"inset-ellipse"` likewise; in each of the three trace axes, `("trace-mean", "line", "after the prior")`, `("trace-band", "band", "after the prior")` and `("trace-truth", "line", "always")`; and `(None, "status", "text", "always")`. Tasks 4 to 6 add theirs.

**The `demo_2d` clip's artists.** Axes, in `fig.axes` order: `"map"`, `"trace-x"`, `"trace-y"`, `"trace-M"`; `"inset"` is a child of `"map"`. `"map"` has both limits at the prior range of x and of y and an equal aspect, on a rectangle that is square in inches. In it: `"dots"` (`draw_dots`, `belief`), row k's `cloud[k][:, 0]` against `cloud[k][:, 1]`, cast to float64; `"clocks"`, a scatter collection of squares at `clock_positions` with `ink` edges and faces from `clock_fill(running_slowing(observations)[k], largest, colors)`, `largest` being the largest finite value of the whole `running_slowing` array; `"truth"` (`draw_truth`) at the true (x, y); `"ellipse"` (`draw_ellipse`, `belief`) holding `ellipse_points(mean[k][:2], cov[k][:2, :2])`; one text each reading `covariance, 2 s.d.` (`belief`) and `stronger fill = slower clock` (`ink`). `"inset"` has the limits `zoom_window` gives for (x, y) at the last row and holds `"inset-dots"`, `"inset-truth"` and `"inset-ellipse"` with the same data as their namesakes, and one text, `zoom_label` of the two magnifications: for each axis, the inset's size as a fraction of the map's (the width or height of its rectangle) times the map's span divided by the window's. The cloud's `zorder` is above the clocks' and below the truth's.

"Built" means `build_clip(replay, colors)` inside `with style(theme) as colors:`, the figure closed in a `finally`; "at row k" means after `clip.show(k)` (with the azimuth of `shots` where the clip has a camera). Unless a test says otherwise the replay is `small(name)` and the theme light. Artists are fetched with `one(axes, gid)` on the axes that owns them, axes with a lookup over `all_axes(fig)` by gid.

  - `test_ellipse_points_lie_two_standard_deviations_out`: `mean = [1.0, -2.0]`, `cov = [[0.04, 0.018], [0.018, 0.09]]`: 121 points, the first equal to the last (`atol=1e-12`), and for every point p, `(p - mean) @ inv(cov) @ (p - mean) == 4` (`rtol=1e-9`); the points' polar angles about `mean`, unwrapped, span a full turn. Only this catches one standard deviation, a diagonal-only covariance (which fails wherever the correlation is not 0), or an arc.

Only this catches a window centred on the mean, one standard deviation instead of two, or a zero-width window.

### Task 4: The two track clips: `demo_1d` and `demo_multi_mass`

**Files:**
- Create: `src/clocks/_clip_tracks.py`
- Modify: `src/clocks/_clips.py`, `tests/_clip_replays.py`, `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`
- Test: `tests/test_clips.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything Task 3 produced in `_clip_parts.py`; `LAYOUTS["demo_1d"]` and `LAYOUTS["demo_multi_mass"]` from Task 1.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_clip_tracks.py`: `LAYOUT_1D` and `LAYOUT_MULTI_1D` (`dict[str, Rect]`); `SIDE_OFFSET: float`, the distance in x between a clock's three marks, from Task 1; `slowing_limits(replay: Replay) -> tuple[float, float]`; `build_1d(replay, colors) -> Clip`; `build_multi_1d(replay, colors) -> Clip`. `BUILDERS` gains both.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_clip_parts.py`: `MASS_LABEL_OFFSETS = ((9, 7), (-9, 7), (9, -7), (-9, -7), (9, 0), (-9, 0), (0, 7), (0, -7))` (points, in the order tried) and `label_masses(ax: Axes, points: Sequence[tuple[float, float]], colors: Palette) -> list[Annotation]`, the probe's rule (decision D51). Called once by a builder after it has shown the final row, it labels point n (from 1) with an annotation `f"mass {n}"` at that point, gid `f"mass-label-{n}"`, in `truth`, 8.5 points, drawn with a halo in the palette's `background` (`path_effects=[patheffects.withStroke(linewidth=3, foreground=colors.background)]`, with `from matplotlib import patheffects` at the top of `_clip_parts.py`) so that it reads over the prior's dots at rows 0 and 1 in both themes (the probe's sheets show it lying over them without one), `textcoords="offset points"`, aligned away from the point (`ha` `"left"`, `"right"` or `"center"` for an x offset above, below or at 0; `va` `"bottom"`, `"top"` or `"center"` likewise). For each offset of `MASS_LABEL_OFFSETS` the label's box is its window extent padded by 2 pixels, and two things are counted: its **exclusions**, one each for the box not lying inside the axes' window extent, overlapping an inset (`ax.child_axes`, by the inset's own unpadded window extent) and overlapping an earlier-placed label (by that label's padded box, as the probe keeps it); and its **marks**, the data points of the axes' own lines (`ax.get_lines()` with data, at the final row, in display coordinates) inside the box. The offset taken is the least by (exclusions, marks, place in `MASS_LABEL_OFFSETS`). This ranks as the probe's 10,000-per-exclusion cost does whenever fewer than 10,000 marks are covered, which holds on every shipped clip, and unlike that cost never lets a candidate with an exclusion beat one without. Labels for mass 1, then mass 2, each drawn above the data (`zorder=6`, as in the probe). The labels then stay where they are for every row. **Fallback:** if every candidate has an exclusion (each leaves the panel, or touches an inset or the other label), the ranking still takes one, the fewest exclusions first; decision D51's guarantee does not hold for that label, and the contact sheets are where it would be seen. `slowing_limits` returns the smallest interval holding `1 - true_rates`, `1 - predicted_mean ± predicted_std` for every row, and `running_slowing(observations) ± noise_std / sqrt(k)` for every row k ≥ 1, widened by 4% of its width on each side.
- `tests/_clip_replays.py`: `DOTS` gains `"demo_1d": [("cloud", "dots", (0, 1), "belief")]` and `"demo_multi_mass": [("cloud", "dots-1", (0, 2), "belief"), ("cloud", "dots-2", (1, 3), "belief_alt")]`; `ELLIPSES` gains `("cloud", "ellipse", (0, 1), "belief")` and `("cloud", "ellipse-1", (0, 2), "belief")`, `("cloud", "ellipse-2", (1, 3), "belief_alt")`; `TRUTHS` the same pairs under `"truth"`, `"truth-1"`, `"truth-2"`; `INSETS` gains `("cloud", "inset", (0, 1), "M", ("dots", "truth", "ellipse"))` and, for the two masses, `("cloud", "inset-1", (0, 2), "M", ("dots-1", "truth-1", "ellipse-1"))` and `("cloud", "inset-2", (1, 3), "M", ("dots-2", "truth-2", "ellipse-2"))`. An inset's copies carry the gid `"<inset gid>-<artist gid>"` (so `"inset-dots"` in the one-mass clips, as in Task 3, and `"inset-1-dots-1"` here); Task 3's inset test reads the names from the table.
- `ARTISTS` gains, for both clips: the clouds, truths, ellipses and inset copies as in Task 3 (with `-1` and `-2` for the two masses); in `"strip"`, `("clocks", "marks", "always")`; in `"slowing"`, `("slowing-truth", "marks", "always")`, `("slowing-predicted", "marks", "always")`, `("slowing-predicted-bar", "segments", "always")`, `("slowing-observed", "marks", "after the prior")`, `("slowing-observed-bar", "segments", "after the prior")`; the traces and the status as in Task 3.

**The track clips' artists.** Axes, in `fig.axes` order: `"cloud"`, `"strip"`, `"slowing"`, then the traces (`"trace-x"`, `"trace-M"`; or `"trace-x₁"`, `"trace-x₂"`, `"trace-M₁"`, `"trace-M₂"`, stacked). `"cloud"` has the prior range of x as its x-limits and the mass prior range as its y-limits; `"strip"` and `"slowing"` have the same x-limits and the same left edge and width as `"cloud"`. In `"cloud"`: the dots, truths, ellipses and insets of the tables above, the second mass drawn with `marker="^"` in `belief_alt` (its ellipse too); one text `covariance, 2 s.d.` (`belief`); for two masses, the labels `mass 1` and `mass 2` that `label_masses` places once, after the final row is shown, each with its `background` halo (decision D51). In `"strip"`: `"clocks"`, a marker-only `Line2D` of `ink` squares at the clocks' x and y = 0. In `"slowing"`, with x the clocks' positions and d = `SIDE_OFFSET`: `"slowing-truth"`, a marker-only `Line2D` (`marker="_"`, `truth`) at (x − d, `1 - true_rates`); `"slowing-predicted"`, a marker-only `Line2D` (`marker="o"`, `belief`) at (x, `1 - predicted_mean[k]`), and `"slowing-predicted-bar"`, the `vlines` collection (`belief`) from that value minus to plus `predicted_std[k]`; `"slowing-observed"`, a marker-only `Line2D` (`marker="s"`, `ink`) at (x + d, `running_slowing(observations)[k]`), and `"slowing-observed-bar"`, the `vlines` collection (`ink`) of half-length `noise_std / sqrt(k)`; the two observed artists are not visible at row 0. `"slowing-observed"` is given its row's data on every row, row 0 included (there `running_slowing` gives NaN for every clock), so at row 0 only its visibility hides it. y-limits `slowing_limits(replay)`; y label `slowing (1 − rate)`.

### Task 5: The two-mass map and the model-comparison clip

**Files:**
- Create: `src/clocks/_clip_models.py`
- Modify: `src/clocks/_clip_maps.py`, `src/clocks/_clips.py`, `tests/_clip_replays.py`, `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`
- Test: `tests/test_clips.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 3's parts; `LAYOUTS["demo_multi_mass_2d"]` and `LAYOUTS["demo_model_comparison"]` from Task 1.
- Produces: in `_clip_maps.py`, `LAYOUT_MULTI_2D` and `build_multi_2d(replay, colors) -> Clip`; in `_clip_models.py`, `LAYOUT_MODELS`, `GUTTER: float` (the least width, in observations, of the room right of T for the final row's labels, from Task 1; widened for a long run, as the artist paragraph says), `LABEL_GAP = 12.0` (points) and `build_models(replay, colors) -> Clip`. `BUILDERS` gains both.
- `tests/_clip_replays.py`: `DOTS` gains `"demo_multi_mass_2d": [("map", "dots-1", (0, 1), "belief"), ("map", "dots-2", (2, 3), "belief_alt")]`; `ELLIPSES` and `TRUTHS` the blocks (0, 1) and (2, 3) under `-1` and `-2` (the ellipses' roles `belief` and `belief_alt`); `INSETS` gains nothing. A new helper `two_candidates() -> Replay`: `small("demo_model_comparison")` with candidate 1 removed (column 0 of `model_prior`, `model_posterior`, `model_log_evidence` and `model_ess` dropped, `model_ks` `[2, 3]`, `model_prior` and each `model_posterior` row divided by its sum, `final_mean_k1` and `final_std_k1` deleted), which the helper passes through `validate_replay`.
- `ARTISTS` gains, for `"demo_multi_mass_2d"`: in `"map"`, the two clouds and two truths as `"marks"`, the two ellipses as `"line"`, `("clocks", "marks", "always")`; the six traces and the status as in Task 3. For `"demo_model_comparison"`, in `"posterior"`, for each K of the small replay: `(f"model-{K}", "line", "after the prior")` and `(f"label-{K}", "text", "always")`; and the status.

**The two-mass map's artists.** Axes: `"map"` (as in Task 3: the prior ranges, equal aspect, square), then six traces in three rows and two columns, `"trace-x₁"`, `"trace-y₁"`, `"trace-M₁"` in the left column and `"trace-x₂"`, `"trace-y₂"`, `"trace-M₂"` in the right. In the map: the dots, truths and ellipses of the tables, the second mass with `marker="^"` in `belief_alt`; the labels `mass 1` and `mass 2` that `label_masses` places once, after the final row is shown, each with its `background` halo (decision D51); `"clocks"`, a marker-only `Line2D` of `ink` squares at `clock_positions`; one text `covariance, 2 s.d.`. No inset. As in Task 3, the clouds' `zorder` is above the clocks' and below the truths'.

**The model-comparison clip's artists.** One axes, `"posterior"`, its limits given below, x label `observations`, y label `posterior probability`. y-limits exactly (−0.03, 1.03), the probe's. For the i-th entry K of `model_ks`: a `Line2D` `f"model-{K}"` in `ordered[i]`, at row k the points 0..k of `model_posterior[:, i]`; and a label `f"label-{K}"` in `ordered[i]` reading `f"K = {K}"`, or `f"K = {K} (true model)"` when K is `meta["true_k"]`, that **follows its line's head** (decision D50): an `Annotation` at `xy = (k, model_posterior[k, i])` with `textcoords="offset points"`, `xytext = (6, dy_i)`, `ha="left"`, `va="center"`, 10 points. No connectors. The vertical shifts `dy_i` keep the labels at least `LABEL_GAP = 12.0` points apart without changing their top-to-bottom order: with `y_i` the head's height in points (its display y from `ax.transData`, times 72 over the figure's dpi), the labels are taken in order of `y_i` from the top, ties in `model_ks` order (the earlier candidate above); the first sits at its head, `q = y`, and each next at `q_j = min(y_j, q_{j-1} - LABEL_GAP)`; then, from the bottom up, the lowest label rises, if it must, to `LABEL_GAP / 2` above the panel's bottom edge, `q = max(q, floor)`, and each label above it only as far as the gap requires, `q_j = max(q_j, q_below + LABEL_GAP)`, so a label whose head is far above is not moved; `dy_i = q_i - y_i`. With these y-limits a head at 1 lies 6.3 points below the panel's top edge and one at 0 the same above its bottom (Q20), so every label stays at least `LABEL_GAP / 2` inside the panel. The x-limits are (0, T + `gutter`), where `gutter = max(GUTTER, T * w / (W - w))`, `W` the panel's width in points and `w` the widest label's width in points (its window extent at the figure's dpi, times 72 over that dpi) plus its 6-point offset and 2 points to spare: `GUTTER` is the least room, and a longer run gets as much more as its widest final label needs to stay inside the panel. `ValueError` matching `at most three candidates` for more than three.

Only this catches a column read by K instead of by position, or lines that start at the first observation.

  - `test_model_labels_follow_the_heads` (each theme), at rows 0, 4 and 9 of the small replay: each label's `xy` is `(row, model_posterior[row, i])`, exactly; its `xyann` is `(6, dy_i)`, with `dy_i` computed in the test by the rule of the artist paragraph from the axes' own `transData` and the figure's dpi (`abs=1e-6`); and the axes hold no artist whose gid starts `connector-`. At row 0 the three heads tie at one third, so the rule stacks the labels 12 points apart in `model_ks` order (shifts 0, −12 and −24 points); at rows 4 and 9 no label moves (Q20). Only this catches labels left in a gutter or at an earlier row's heads, a label on another candidate's head, ties ordered the other way, or the connectors kept.

Only this catches the rise at the panel's bottom being left out, which no row of the small replay needs (Q20), or a rise applied to every label alike, which on that case pushes the top label 5.3 points out of the panel (measured on the probe's panel).

### Task 6: The echolocation clip and its camera

**Files:**
- Create: `src/clocks/_clip_echo.py`
- Modify: `src/clocks/_clips.py`, `tests/_clip_replays.py`, `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`
- Test: `tests/test_clips.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 3's parts; `LAYOUTS["demo_echolocation_3d"]` from Task 1.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_clip_echo.py`: `LAYOUT_ECHO`; `ELEVATION = 18.0`; `azimuth(frame: int, frames: int) -> float`, the design's `-75 + 40 * (1 - cos(pi * frame / (frames - 1))) / 2`; `in_view(points: NDArray, bounds: Sequence[float]) -> NDArray[np.bool_]` (true where x, y and z all lie in the closed interval, as `weight_in_view` counts them); `view_weight_text(row: int, weight: float) -> str`; `build_echo(replay, colors) -> Clip`. `BUILDERS` gains it.
- `shots(replay)` for `demo_echolocation_3d` (in `_clips.py`): with `dwell = dwell_frames(T)` and `F = sum(dwell[:-1])`, one `Shot(row, 1, azimuth(f, F))` for each video frame f of rows 0 to T − 1, in order, then `Shot(T, dwell[-1], azimuth(F - 1, F))`. For every other demo, unchanged.
- `tests/_clip_replays.py`: `DOTS` gains `"demo_echolocation_3d": [("overview", "overview-dots", (0, 2), "belief")]`.
- `ARTISTS` gains, for `"demo_echolocation_3d"`: in `"scene"`, `("dots", "marks", "when any is in view")`, `("clocks", "marks", "always")`, `("truth", "marks", "always")`, `("truth-drop", "line", "always")`, `("head", "segments", "always")`; in `"overview"`, `("overview-dots", "marks", "always")` and `("view-box", "line", "always")`; in `"fit"`, `("fit-points", "marks", "after the prior")`, `("fit-diagonal", "line", "always")` and `("fit-empty", "text", "only the prior")`; the four traces as in Task 3; and the figure-level `(None, "status", "text", "always")` and `(None, "view-weight", "text", "always")`. The close view's cloud is not in `DOTS` (its data are a subset of a row), so this table is what requires it to be visible. Its "when" is not one of the three but a fourth, `"when any is in view"`, which only this entry uses: `test_listed_artists_are_drawn` requires it shown at a row where some dot of that row lies inside `view_bounds` (by `in_view`), and hidden or empty otherwise. The small recording's prior row has no dot inside the cube here (Q9: 0 of 40), but `view_bounds` comes from the run's own path, and a recording made on another processor can differ in low-order bits (the note on `assert_computed_input_matches` in `tests/test_replays.py`), so the test does not assume it; the committed prior row has 2 of 500, and a test of its own, below, requires those to be drawn.

**The echolocation clip's artists.** Axes, in `fig.axes` order: `"scene"` (3D), `"overview"`, `"fit"`, `"trace-x"`, `"trace-y"`, `"trace-z"`, `"trace-M"`. Figure-level texts: `"status"` and, under it, `"view-weight"`, reading `view_weight_text(k, weight_in_view[k])`: `f"{100 * w:.1f}% of the prior lies inside the close view"` at row 0 and `f"{100 * w:.1f}% of the cloud's weight lies inside the close view"` afterwards.
  - `"scene"`: all three limits are `view_bounds`; the camera is `view_init(elev=18, azim=<the shot's azimuth>)`, and `azimuth(F - 1, F)` when none is given. `"head"`, the twelve edges of the clocks' bounding box, a `Line3DCollection` in `muted` built from the twelve end-point pairs and added with `scene.add_collection3d` (`_clip_echo` imports the class by name, `from mpl_toolkits.mplot3d.art3d import Line3DCollection`, so that a test can capture what it is given); `"clocks"`, a marker-only `Line3D` of `ink` squares at `clock_positions`; `"dots"`, a marker-only `Line3D` in `belief` holding exactly the points of `cloud[k]` for which `in_view` is true, in archive order; `"truth"`, an open ring (`marker="o"`, face `"none"`, edge `truth` with a width above 0) at the true (x, y, z), larger than the dots; `"truth-drop"`, a `Line3D` in `truth` from the truth straight down to z = `view_bounds[0]`. Panes, pane edges, grid and axis lines take palette colours.
  - `"overview"`: both limits the prior range of x and of z, equal aspect; `"overview-dots"`, `cloud[k][:, 0]` against `cloud[k][:, 2]`; `"view-box"`, a closed `Line2D` in `muted` around the square from `view_bounds[0]` to `view_bounds[1]`.
  - `"fit"`: equal limits on both axes, fixed, holding for every row k ≥ 1 the running mean of the centred readings, `observations[:k].mean(axis=0)`, and `predicted_mean[k]`; x label `observed mean`, y label `fitted`. `"fit-points"`, a marker-only `Line2D` in `ink`, one point per clock, those two quantities; `"fit-diagonal"`, a `Line2D` in `muted` between the two corners of the limits; `"fit-empty"`, a text `no readings yet`. At row 0 the text is visible and the points are not; afterwards the reverse.

### Task 7: The rendered-site check learns clips

No page changes: the manifest still lists plots only, and no build ships media until Task 10, so the real site passes as before. The clip branch is exercised by fixtures here and by the real site from Task 10.

**Files:**
- Modify: `scripts/check_site_figures.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock` (`uv add --dev pillow`: the checker now imports Pillow by name, decision D40)
- Test: `tests/test_check_site_figures.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `probe`, `dwell_frames`, `find_encoder`, `EncoderNotFound`, `FPS` from `clocks._video`; `load_replay`, `save_replay` from `clocks._replay`; `Image` and `UnidentifiedImageError` from Pillow.
- Produces, in `scripts/check_site_figures.py`:
  - `ARCHIVES = ROOT / "assets" / "replays"`; `MEDIA = "media"`, the directory of the output that holds every clip and poster; `HIDING_CLASSES = frozenset({"d-none", "hidden", "invisible", "visually-hidden", "collapse"})`; `REPLAY_CSS = b"figure.replay video { max-width: 100%; height: auto; }\n"`, the whole content of the stylesheet a page with a clip must link.
  - `_FigureParser` also collects: `themed`, every themed element in document order, each a plot wrapper (`_Wrapper`, as today) or a clip rendering (`_Video`: its themes, its attributes as a dict in which each of the boolean attributes `controls`, `playsinline`, `autoplay` and `loop` counts as present when its key is there, whatever its value: `None` (written bare, as in the source block), `""` (as Quarto writes it: decision D22) or any other (`autoplay="true"`), as HTML reads a boolean attribute, the index of its replay figure, and `hidden_by`, below); `replay_captions`, one list of caption texts per `<figure>` whose class list holds `replay` (which is what makes it a replay figure); `replay_classes`, the class list of each such figure; and `texts`, a dict from each `id` on the page to a list with, for each element carrying it, its whitespace-normalised text and its `hidden_by`. A themed `<video>` that opens inside a replay figure and outside any plot wrapper is a clip rendering; **any other themed element is a plot wrapper**, as section 10 says. A `figcaption` inside a replay figure belongs to that figure. The scan of pages the manifest does not list counts `themed`, so a clip's two videos there are "2 theme wrapper(s)" in today's message.
  - **`hidden_by`**: the first of an element's ancestors, or the element itself, that is *marked hidden*, as the text of its start tag, or `None`. An element is marked hidden when its class list holds one of `HIDING_CLASSES`; or it has the attribute `hidden`; or `aria-hidden="true"`; or its `style` attribute, lower-cased and with all whitespace removed, contains `display:none` or `visibility:hidden`; or it is a `<template>` or a `<noscript>`; or it is a `<details>` without the attribute `open`. Ancestry is tracked as wrappers are today, by counting the marked element's own tag. These are the markers of decision D39 and the whole of what the check refuses; it is not a computation of visibility (see "The limit", below). `_FigureParser` also collects `stylesheets`, the `href` of every `<link>` whose `rel`, split on whitespace and lower-cased (HTML reads it without regard to case), holds `stylesheet`. An element is *not* marked hidden by an `open` attribute's value: a `<details>` with `open` present, whatever its value (`open="false"` included), is open, as HTML reads a boolean attribute.
  - `_site_path(src: str, page_path: Path, output_dir: Path, what: str = "image") -> tuple[Path | None, str]`: today's `_resolve_source` up to and not including its file-existence check, moved out whole, with today's rule that an empty decoded path names no file kept in it (`f"{what} {src!r} does not exist"`, today's message; `_site_path` returns a resolved `Path`, which no longer shows that the decoded string was empty): a URL `urlsplit` cannot parse is `f"{what} URL is malformed: ..."`; a decoded path holding a NUL is `f"{what} path contains a NUL: ..."`; a scheme, a network location, a decoded path that starts with `/`, or a resolved path outside the output directory is `f"{what} is outside the site: ..."`; otherwise the resolved path and `""`. `_resolve_source(src, page_path, output_dir, what="image")` calls it and then makes today's existence check, with `what` as the first word of each message (`"video"`, `"poster"`). The rules, the messages and pull request 2's tests of them are unchanged.
  - `_links_the_replay_stylesheet(hrefs: Sequence[str], page_path: Path, output_dir: Path) -> bool`: whether `_site_path(href, page_path, output_dir, "stylesheet")` gives, for some `href`, the path `(output_dir / "replay.css").resolve()`. An `href` that `_site_path` refuses (malformed, holding a NUL, with a scheme or host, starting with `/`, or leading out of the site) never counts, and is not a failure of its own: what fails is a page with no `href` that counts. The file need not exist for this; its content is a check of its own. No stylesheet is parsed anywhere in the checker (decision D45).
  - `_read_poster(path: Path) -> tuple[tuple[int, int] | None, str | None]`, with Pillow and no other import: the poster's pixel size and what is wrong with it, one of them `None`. The problem is `f"cannot be read: {type(error).__name__}"` when the first `Image.open` raises an `OSError` other than `UnidentifiedImageError` (an unreadable file is a failure of the check, not a traceback); `"cannot be opened as a PNG"` when it raises `UnidentifiedImageError` or reports a format other than `PNG`; `f"does not decode: {type(error).__name__}"` when `verify()` raises, or when a second `Image.open` followed by `load()` raises (`OSError`, `SyntaxError`, `ValueError` or `zlib.error`). Otherwise the result is the size, read from the image whose pixels were loaded, and no problem. Both steps are needed (Q15): `verify()` alone passes invalid filter bytes, and `load()` alone passes a file with no `IEND`.
  - `figure_failures(output_dir: Path) -> list[str]`, signature unchanged. `EXPECTED_FIGURES` is unchanged in this task.

**What the check does with a clip.** In `_page_failures`, after the both-classes, nesting and count checks (which now count every themed element; the count message keeps its words), figure `i`'s two themed elements are checked in this order, and the first failure of the first two ends the checks on that figure:
  1. Kind: `f"{page}: figure {i}: expected a clip, found a plot wrapper"` or `f"{page}: figure {i}: expected a plot, found a clip video"`.
  2. Order: today's `expected light-content then dark-content, found ...`.
  3. A `Fig` whose kind is neither `"plot"` nor `"clip"` raises `ValueError(f"{page}: figure {i}: no check for kind {kind!r}")`, as today; this is decided first, from the manifest alone, before steps 1 and 2 look at the page, so an unknown kind is never reported as a kind mismatch.
  4. For a clip, with `label = f"{page}: figure {i}"`, `h = figure.height` and `clip = figure.clip`:
     - `f"{label}: the two videos are not in one <figure class=\"replay\">"`, and nothing more for this figure;
     - `f"{label}: the replay figure's classes are {sorted(classes)}, expected ['figure', 'replay']"` unless its class list is exactly `replay` and `figure`, in either order (decision D22: as Quarto 1.11.9 writes it);
     - `f"{label}: the clip is inside a hidden element: {start_tag}"` when either video's `hidden_by` is set (once, for the first);
     - the figure's captions: `f"{label}: expected 1 figcaption, found {n}"`, or `f"{label}: empty caption"`;
     - per theme, with `t = f"{label} {theme}-content"`: `f"{t}: width {value}, expected 750"`; `f"{t}: height {value}, expected {h}"`; `f"{t}: no controls attribute"`; `f"{t}: no playsinline attribute"`; `f"{t}: preload {value!r}, expected 'none'"`; `f"{t}: autoplay is set"`; `f"{t}: loop is set"`; `f"{t}: empty aria-label"`; then one of `f"{t}: no aria-describedby"` (the attribute absent or blank), `f"{t}: aria-describedby {name!r} names no element on the page"`, `f"{t}: aria-describedby {name!r} names {n} elements"`, `f"{t}: the description {name!r} is empty"`, `f"{t}: the description {name!r} is inside a hidden element: {start_tag}"`;
     - per theme, the files: `_resolve_source` on `src` (`what="video"`) and on `poster` (`what="poster"`); then, comparing the resolved file with the one intended, `f"{t}: video is {relative!r}, expected {MEDIA}/{clip}.{theme}.mp4"` and `f"{t}: poster is {relative!r}, expected {MEDIA}/{clip}.{theme}.png"`, `relative` being the resolved file's path from the output directory; `f"{t}: poster {problem}"` when `_read_poster` reports a problem, else `f"{t}: poster is {w} by {h2} pixels, expected 1500 by {2 * h}"` from the size it returns;
     - per theme, the decoded stream, when the video file is the intended one and an encoder was found: `f"{t}: video cannot be decoded: {error}"` when `probe` raises `ValueError`; else each of `f"{t}: video codec {codec}, expected h264"`, `f"{t}: video pixel format {pix_fmt}, expected yuv420p"`, `f"{t}: video is {w} by {h2} pixels, expected 1500 by {2 * h}"`, `f"{t}: video runs at {fps:g} frames per second, expected {FPS}"` (the rate compared with `FPS`), `f"{t}: video decodes to {frames} frames, expected {n}"`, `f"{t}: video has mdat before moov"` that applies. `n` is `sum(dwell_frames(T))` for the `n_observations` of `ARCHIVES / f"{clip}.npz"`, read **once per clip figure**, before its per-theme stream checks and only when an encoder was found; an archive that cannot be read gives the one failure `f"{label}: no readable archive for clip {clip!r}: {error}"` for the figure, and neither theme's frame count is compared;
     - the pair: `f"{label}: aria-describedby differs between themes: {a!r} vs {b!r}"`; `f"{label}: light and dark use the same video file"`, else `f"{label}: light and dark videos have the same content"`; the same two for posters, with `poster` for `video`. Comparing the two files of one build with each other is what the design asks; no file is compared with a stored copy.

  Three checks are made once per site, and one once per page with a clip:
  - **The published media set**, always: every entry directly inside `output_dir / MEDIA` (when that directory exists) must be one of `<clip>.<theme>.mp4` and `<clip>.<theme>.png` for a clip the manifest lists; each other entry gives `f"{MEDIA}/{name}: not one of the site's clips and posters"`. Missing files are reported where an embed names them.
  - **The encoder**, when the manifest lists a clip: it is looked up before any page is read, and `EncoderNotFound` gives the single failure `f"clips cannot be decoded: {error}"`; the other clip checks still run.
  - **The stylesheet**, when the manifest lists a clip: `"replay.css: missing from the site"` when `output_dir / "replay.css"` is not a file, else `"replay.css: not the canonical rule"` unless its bytes equal `REPLAY_CSS`. And for each page whose manifest entry lists a clip: `f"{page}: no stylesheet link to replay.css"` unless `_links_the_replay_stylesheet` holds for the page's `stylesheets`.

**The limit, written in the script's docstring and the test file.** The check refuses a clip or a description whose own start tag, or an ancestor's, carries one of the markers decision D39 names, a clip file anywhere but `media/`, a poster that does not decode and a stream that does not decode to the expected frames. It does not compute visibility: a stylesheet rule, a script, an ancestor's opacity, an ancestor that clips it (`clip-path`, or `overflow: hidden` on a box too small to hold it) or any marker outside D39's list can still hide a clip, and only the browser check of Task 13, which looks at the painted page, finds those. It establishes that the page links a `replay.css` holding exactly the canonical rule, not that the rule takes effect: another stylesheet or a script can override it, and the 390-pixel browser check of Task 13 is what shows a clip shrinking with its column. It establishes that the clip has the frame count of its archive's schedule, not that its frames show that archive; the full render, which redraws every clip from `assets/replays/` and whose failure fails the build (Task 1, item g), is what ties the published files to their inputs (decision D27).

### Task 8: The pre-render step's library function and script

This task adds the function and the three-line script and tests them. Quarto is not told to run the script, and no media ships, until Task 10 turns both on together with the embeds (decision D38): no build publishes a file that no page shows.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/clocks/_site_media.py`, `site/prerender.py`, `tests/test_site_media.py`
- Modify: `.gitignore`, `tests/_clip_replays.py`, `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `CLIP_HEIGHTS`, `render_clip`, `final_frame` from `clocks._clips`; `find_encoder`, `encoder_version` from `clocks._video`; `load_replay`, `DEMOS` from `clocks._replay`; `style`, `save_png`, `THEMES` from `clocks._style`.
- Produces, in `src/clocks/_site_media.py`:

```python
CLIP_DEMOS: tuple[str, ...]  # DEMOS without "demo_density", in DEMOS order
PACKAGE: Path                # the directory this module is in
STAGED = ".staged-"          # the prefix of every name this step stages under

def media_paths(media: Path, demo: str, theme: str) -> tuple[Path, Path]: ...
def render_site_media(archives: Path, media: Path, *, full: bool,
                      demos: Sequence[str] = CLIP_DEMOS, sources: Path = PACKAGE,
                      report: Callable[[str], None] = print) -> list[Path]: ...
```

  **The media directory belongs wholly to this step.** Nothing else writes there, it is git-ignored, and two runs at once in one directory are not supported. `media_paths` returns `(media / f"{demo}.{theme}.mp4", media / f"{demo}.{theme}.png")`. `render_site_media`, in this order:
  1. finds the encoder; `EncoderNotFound` propagates before anything is created, written or removed. Reports `f"encoder: {encoder} ({encoder_version(encoder)})"`.
  2. creates `media` if missing, and removes every regular file directly inside it whose name starts with `STAGED`: what an interrupted earlier run left. `clocks._video.encode` names its own temporary file by appending to the name it is given, so those start with `STAGED` too. No other name is claimed as a leftover: a `.tmp` file of another program's is not this step's.
  3. for each demo, then each theme of `THEMES`: the pair is *stale* when `full` is true, or either file is missing, or the older of the two is not strictly newer than both `archives / f"{demo}.npz"` and the newest regular file under `sources` (directories named `__pycache__` skipped). (Superseded after the review's round 4 by the content rule of D15.) A stale pair is rebuilt in three stages:
     - **stage**: `render_clip(replay, staged_clip, theme=theme, encoder=encoder)`, then `save_png(final_frame(replay, colors), staged_poster)` inside `with style(theme) as colors:`, the figure closed in a `finally`; the staged names are the final names with `STAGED` in front. A failure here, the encoder's included, removes the pair's staged files and propagates; the old pair, if any, is exactly as it was.
     - **retire**: both old final files are removed.
     - **publish**: `os.replace(staged_clip, clip)`, then `os.replace(staged_poster, poster)`.
     An interruption between retiring and the end of publishing leaves a file missing, never a new clip beside an old poster: the pair is stale on the next run, and the rendered-site check fails on the missing file meanwhile. Reports `f"rendered {clip.name} ({frames} frames, {seconds:.1f} s)"`, or `f"kept {clip.name}"`.
  4. when `full`, removes every regular file directly inside `media` whose suffix is `.mp4` or `.png` and which step 3 did not just write, reporting `f"removed {name}"`. It never enters a subdirectory and never removes a file with another suffix.
  5. returns the paths it wrote, clip then poster, in the order written.

  What it does not do: a render that is not full keeps every other clip and poster already in the directory, current or not. Only a full render followed by the rendered-site check, which requires the output's `media/` to hold exactly the expected files (Task 7), establishes the published set.
- `site/prerender.py`, whole:

```python
"""Quarto pre-render step: draw the site's clips and posters into media/."""

import os
from pathlib import Path

from clocks._site_media import render_site_media

SITE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
render_site_media(
    SITE.parent / "assets" / "replays",
    SITE / "media",
    full=os.environ.get("QUARTO_PROJECT_RENDER_ALL") == "1",
)
```

- `.gitignore` gains `site/media/` under "Quarto build products".

Helpers and their tests only. The table of real embeds is empty until Task 10, so every page still passes.

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `CLIP_HEIGHTS` from `clocks._clips`; `Fig`, `EXPECTED_FIGURES` from the script, as today.
- Produces, in `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`:

```python
class Embed(NamedTuple):
    clip: str
    label: str    # the aria-label of both videos
    caption: str

REPLAY_EMBEDS: dict[str, tuple[Embed, ...]] = {}  # page, relative to site/, to its embeds in order
FIGURE_PAGES: tuple[str, ...]                      # sorted(set(PLOT_PAGES) | set(REPLAY_EMBEDS))

def _description_id(clip: str) -> str: ...        # "demo_2d" -> "demo-2d-text"
def _embed_block(embed: Embed, page: str) -> str: ...
def _replay_embed_violations(text: str, page: str, embeds: Sequence[Embed]) -> list[str]: ...
def _descriptions(text: str) -> dict[str, str]: ...
def _page_figures(text: str, page: str) -> list[tuple[str, int, str | None]]: ...
def _without(description: str, sentences: Sequence[str]) -> str: ...

STRAY = re.compile(r"[0-9₀-₉]")  # a digit, ASCII or subscript; Task 10 scans with it
```

  - `_embed_block(embed, page)` returns exactly this text, with `{id}` from `_description_id`, `{h}` from `CLIP_HEIGHTS[embed.clip]` and `{up}` being `"../"` once per directory level of `page` (`""` for `index.qmd`):

````text
```{=html}
<figure class="replay">
<video class="light-content" aria-label="{label}" aria-describedby="{id}" src="{up}media/{clip}.light.mp4" poster="{up}media/{clip}.light.png" width="750" height="{h}" controls playsinline preload="none"></video>
<video class="dark-content" aria-label="{label}" aria-describedby="{id}" src="{up}media/{clip}.dark.mp4" poster="{up}media/{clip}.dark.png" width="750" height="{h}" controls playsinline preload="none"></video>
<figcaption>{caption}</figcaption>
</figure>
```
````

  - `_replay_embed_violations(text, page, embeds)`: for embed number n (from 1), `f"embed {n} ({clip}): the pinned block is missing or altered"` when `_embed_block` does not occur in `text`, or `f"embed {n} ({clip}): the pinned block occurs {k} times"`; `"the embeds are out of order"` when all occur once but not in the table's order; for each embed found once, `f"embed {n} ({clip}): not followed by its description `::: {{#{id}}}`"` unless the next non-blank line after the block is exactly `::: {#<id>}`, or `f"embed {n} ({clip}): the description is empty"` when no non-blank line lies between that line and the closing `:::`, or `f"embed {n} ({clip}): the description id occurs {k} times"` when `{#<id>}` occurs more than once in `text`; and, in the text with every pinned block removed, one `f"unpinned video markup: line {m}"` for each line (numbered in the original text) that contains, in any letter case, `<video`, `<source`, `<figure`, `<iframe`, `.mp4` or `{=html}`. It has no rule about what surrounds a block: whether Markdown renders the block is the rendered-site check's to establish, and so is whether the result sits under one of the hiding markers decision D39 names; any other way of hiding it is found only by the browser check of Task 13 (decision D23).
  - `_descriptions(text)` maps the id of each `::: {#<id>}` div to its body, whitespace-normalised.
  - `_page_figures(text, page)` returns, in source order, `("plot", _cell_height(cell), None)` for each plot cell and `("clip", CLIP_HEIGHTS[clip], clip)` for each of the page's pinned blocks.
  - `_without(description, sentences)` returns the description with each of `sentences` removed, and raises `AssertionError` naming the first sentence that is not in the description. There is no list of allowed leftovers: a description's every number is inside a sentence the test builds.

It does not check what surrounds a block, so it does not catch a canonical block inside a code fence or an HTML comment, which Markdown does not render, or inside a wrapper that hides it. The rendered-site check (`scripts/check_site_figures.py`) catches the first, and part of the second: a block that was not rendered leaves no video, and the count of themed elements against the manifest fails; a wrapper carrying one of the markers that script names (`HIDING_CLASSES`, `hidden`, `aria-hidden`, two inline styles, `template`, `noscript`, a closed `details`) fails its hidden-ancestor rule. A wrapper that hides by any other means, an opacity of zero or a `clip-path` for two, passes both. Actual visibility, keyboard reach and playback are browser checks, made by hand for each pull request that changes a clip or its page."

### Task 10: The switch: seven embeds, six descriptions, and the GIFs retired

One change, so that every sentence is true at its commit: Quarto is told to run the pre-render step and to ship its media, the pages show the clips, the manifest lists them, the GIFs and everything on the site that names them go, and the provenance prose describes what is there, including what the commands still do.

- Create: `site/replay.css`

```python
REPLAY_EMBEDS = {
    "index.qmd": (Embed(
        "demo_2d", "Animation: the 2D search",
        "One simulated run: a particle filter finding a hidden mass on a plane from "
        "clock rates alone. Left: the cloud of hypotheses, the eight clocks and the "
        "true position. Right: the estimates of x, y and M after each observation."),),
    "story/the-search-in-one-dimension.qmd": (Embed(
        "demo_1d", "Animation: the search in one dimension",
        "The 1D search, one simulated run. Left: the cloud of hypotheses in position "
        "and mass, above the three clocks and, clock by clock, the true, predicted "
        "and measured slowing. Right: the estimates of x and M after each observation."),),
    "story/into-the-plane.qmd": (Embed(
        "demo_2d", "Animation: the 2D search",
        "The 2D search, one simulated run: 8 clocks, one hidden mass, three unknowns "
        "(x, y, M). Left: the cloud and the clocks, each given a stronger fill the "
        "slower it runs. Right: the three estimates after each observation."),),
    "story/two-hidden-masses.qmd": (
        Embed("demo_multi_mass", "Animation: two masses on a line",
              "Two masses in 1D: five clocks, four unknowns. One simulated run."),
        Embed("demo_multi_mass_2d", "Animation: two masses on a plane",
              "Two masses in 2D: ten randomly placed clocks, six unknowns. "
              "One simulated run."),
    ),
    "story/how-many-masses.qmd": (Embed(
        "demo_model_comparison",
        "Animation: the probability of one, two and three masses",
        "One simulated run: posterior probability over K=1, 2, 3 as 80 observations "
        "accumulate. Truth is K=2."),),
    "story/gravitational-echolocation.qmd": (Embed(
        "demo_echolocation_3d", "Animation: the head locating an exterior mass",
        "The echolocation demo, one simulated run. Left: the head, its clocks, the "
        "cloud and the true mass (ring), with the whole search box at upper left. "
        "Right: the four estimates after each observation, and the fitted readings "
        "against the observed ones: agreement with the readings used in the fit, "
        "not a test on new data."),),
}

EXPECTED_FIGURES = {
    "index.html": (Fig("clip", 450, "demo_2d"),),
    "story/clocks-as-gravimeters.html": (Fig("plot", 420),),
    "story/one-clock-is-not-enough.html": (Fig("plot", 420),),
    "story/the-search-in-one-dimension.html": (Fig("clip", 450, "demo_1d"), Fig("plot", 540)),
    "story/into-the-plane.html": (Fig("clip", 450, "demo_2d"), Fig("plot", 540)),
    "story/two-hidden-masses.html": (
        Fig("clip", 500, "demo_multi_mass"), Fig("clip", 550, "demo_multi_mass_2d"),
        Fig("plot", 420),
    ),
    "story/how-many-masses.html": (Fig("clip", 420, "demo_model_comparison"), Fig("plot", 420)),
    "story/beyond-point-masses.html": (Fig("plot", 540), Fig("plot", 420)),
    "story/gravitational-echolocation.html": (
        Fig("plot", 420), Fig("clip", 550, "demo_echolocation_3d"), Fig("plot", 540),
    ),
    "method/the-particle-filter.html": (Fig("plot", 540),),
}
```

  The captions' layout words ("Left", "Right", "at upper left") follow the design's arrangement; if Task 1's layout differs, the caption follows the layout and Task 13 checks it by eye.

**The six descriptions.** Each goes in a `::: {#<id>}` div directly under its block. A number is written with `trace_decimals` of its parameter's prior range (two decimals for positions, three for masses), a minus sign as the ASCII hyphen. The text is given here with today's numbers (Q8); the tests below build the numbered sentences from the archives.

  - `demo-1d-text`: "Before any reading the dots cover the whole search: every position along the track and every mass the prior allows. The first reading pulls them into a narrow band around the true position, and each of the next few tightens it; under the cloud, the filter's predicted slowing at each clock closes on the measured one. After 50 readings the run ends at x = 2.48 ± 0.04 and M = 0.101 ± 0.001, against a true x = 2.50 and M = 0.100. Each ± is one standard deviation of the final cloud: how far the surviving hypotheses still disagree in this one run, not an error bar over many runs."
  - `demo-2d-text`, the same words on the landing page and on "Into the Plane": "Before any reading the dots cover the whole square. The first readings pull them toward the mass, and by the eighth they are a small cloud around it; the three traces at the right narrow at the same time. After 50 readings the run ends at x = 1.43 ± 0.07, y = -1.15 ± 0.08 and M = 0.149 ± 0.001, against a true x = 1.50, y = -1.00 and M = 0.150. Each ± is one standard deviation of the final cloud. The y estimate sits about two of those from the truth: that is this run's noise, and another seed would miss in another direction."
  - `demo-multi-mass-text`: "Before any reading both clouds are spread along the whole track. The first mass is narrowed at once; the second takes longer: after one reading its position is x₂ = 6.61 ± 1.04, and after eight it is 4.62 ± 0.18. After 80 readings the run ends at x₁ = -2.00 ± 0.04, x₂ = 4.55 ± 0.05, M₁ = 0.045 ± 0.001 and M₂ = 0.030 ± 0.001, against true values of -2.00, 4.50, 0.045 and 0.030. Each ± is one standard deviation of the final cloud for that parameter."
  - `demo-multi-mass-2d-text`: "Before any reading both clouds cover the whole square. The first readings barely narrow them, and for a while each cloud is split between two arrangements of the masses, which the ellipses cannot show and the dots can. After 80 readings the run ends at x₁ = -2.38 ± 0.42, y₁ = 2.03 ± 0.22, x₂ = 4.13 ± 0.80, y₂ = -0.95 ± 0.63, M₁ = 0.052 ± 0.004 and M₂ = 0.028 ± 0.003, against true values of -3.00, 2.00, 4.00, -1.00, 0.050 and 0.030. The final clouds are still wide: this run locates both masses loosely, and each ± is one standard deviation of its final cloud." The clause about two arrangements is the design's reading of this run ("the cloud passes through a second mode"); it is believed, not measured, and Task 13 removes it if the sheets do not show it.
  - `demo-model-comparison-text`: the paragraph already under the figure, unchanged, wrapped in the div: "One mass is all but ruled out by the first observation. Three masses lead at first, two take over around the tenth observation, and from about the twentieth three stay ahead: the run ends at about 40% for two masses and 60% for three, leaning toward the wrong answer. That is one run's estimate from finite data and a finite particle cloud, a weak preference rather than a verdict."
  - `demo-echolocation-3d-text`: "Before any reading the dots fill the whole search box, 32 units on a side, and only 0.4% of the prior lies inside the close view around the head. The first reading collapses the cloud into that view, where 100.0% of its weight stays; the small panel of the whole box shows one cluster where the box was full. After 80 readings the run ends at x = 0.98 ± 0.02, y = 1.46 ± 0.02, z = 2.93 ± 0.03 and M = 0.078 ± 0.002, against true values of 0.99, 1.48, 2.97 and 0.080. Each ± is one standard deviation of the final cloud. The panel of fitted against observed readings shows agreement with the readings used in the fit, not a test on new data."

### Task 11: The 2D demo's true mass, 0.15 to 0.10, and the box-margin guard

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/clocks/_demos/demo_2d.py`, `assets/replays/demo_2d.npz` (re-recorded), `site/index.qmd`, `site/story/into-the-plane.qmd`, `docs/audits/2026-11-17/README.md`
- Test: `tests/test_replays.py`, `tests/test_site_figure_style.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces, in `tests/test_replays.py`: `BOX_MARGIN = 0.05`; `_box_margins(true_params: NDArray, prior_ranges: Sequence[Sequence[float]], dimensions: int) -> NDArray[np.float64]`, per parameter the smaller of its distances to the two edges of its prior range, divided by the range's width. ### Task 14: Mutation checks

The run changes no file. This task proves the new tests can fail; a fix that it leads to is made in the task that owns the code (Step 3). **The coordinator runs it, with a mutation harness in worktrees that no reviewer or implementer is using.** The harness may run mutants in several worktrees at once, one mutant per worktree at a time; each row stays one mutant and one whole-suite run, and no row is dropped or merged to save time (216 rows, some of them several mutants, at over three minutes each: Q10). In each worktree the harness syncs the environment once on the clean tree (`uv sync --frozen`), then for every mutant sets `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, deletes every `__pycache__`, confirms `git status --porcelain` is empty, applies the mutant and restores it in a `try`/`finally`, and runs **the whole suite, default and slow tests together, with `uv run --no-sync pytest -q -m ''`**. **Each mutant's run has a time limit of its own**: three times the clean baseline's duration, measured in Step 1 (about ten minutes at Q10's 3 min 19 s), enforced by the harness, which starts each run in a new process group and kills the whole group when the limit passes, so that no hung encoder or test outlives its row. Such a row is classed "timed out" (Step 3); no row may be declared knowing that it hangs, which is why the table has no mutant that waits for the encoder before closing its input on the successful path. The coordinator's own waiting on the harness follows Global Constraints. A harness that runs several worktrees at once waits on each worker's own process (its PID, or the session it started), never with the `pgrep` loop of Global Constraints, which would also see another worktree's pytest. `--no-sync` matters: a mutant that edits `pyproject.toml` must fail its test, not be absorbed or rejected by a re-resolution.

**The expected sets are declared before the run, for this pull request's head.** The table below is that declaration. Before the first mutant the coordinator records the baseline (every test passing, and the list of collected IDs) and checks each row's names against that list: a row naming a test that does not exist is corrected, with a note, before any mutant runs. Every mutant breaks library code, a script, a page, a configuration file or a data file, never a test or a test helper. An ID in square brackets is a parametrized case; "each clip" means the six clips' cases, "each theme" both. Where a mutant names a part shared by several clips, it is applied in the shared part. A cell does not claim that its tests are the only tests of that behaviour, only that these and no others fail for this one edit.

|---|---|---|
| 2 | `dwell_frames`: the counts for observations 4 to 8 and 9 to 20 exchanged | `test_dwell_frames[50]`, `[80]`, `[9]`, `[21]`; `test_echo_shots_turn_the_camera_and_hold` |
| 2 | `dwell_frames(0)` returns `[24]` | `test_dwell_frames_needs_an_observation` |
