What the table rests on. The three existing failures of the `TRUE_M` row were measured (Q7); every other expected set is derived from the tests as this plan specifies them, and from the existing tests as read. Two kinds of row are the least certain, and a difference there is read as a finding about the plan before it is read as one about the code: rows on a shared part, whose per-clip cases depend on which clips the implementer routes through it; and the `_style.py` row, which assumes `save_png` and `frame_rgba` share one function as Task 2 requires. The three rows that end "writes that list out from the baseline" are completed by the coordinator before the run, as the paragraph above requires, so no row's expectation is filled in after its result is known. Not every check of Task 7 has a row: the nesting and both-classes checks and `_resolve_source`'s rules are pull request 2's, with that pull request's mutants, and are reused here unchanged apart from the `what` word and their move into `_site_path`; so dropping a query, refusing a leading `/` and the other URL rules, which the stylesheet link now shares, have no new row, and the stylesheet's own fixtures (`link_with_a_query`, `root_relative_link`, `malformed_link`, `nul_in_link`) show that the link goes through them. Some behaviours have no mutant, each for a stated reason: the encoder's and decoder's standard error going to a file (a blocked pipe hangs the suite instead of failing it); on the successful path, the encoder waited for only after its input is closed (a wait before the close hangs every real encode, since FFmpeg exits only at end of input, so no whole-suite run could finish; `test_run_encoder_reaps_the_process[success]` pins the order, and the row for the broken-pipe path tests the same rule where FFmpeg is expected to have exited already, with the time limit for any other outcome); the numbers in the layout tables (decision D20: the containment, overlap and fixity tests catch a broken table, and the sheets a bad one); the page-source guard's helpers (test code, exercised here by the page mutants of Task 10; what the guard does not catch by design is in decision D23); the contact sheets; and the browser checks.

- [ ] **Step 1: Complete and check the declaration**, as above, against the baseline's collected IDs.
- [ ] **Step 2: Run each mutation** and record, for each, the exact set of failing test IDs and any change in what is collected.
- [ ] **Step 3: Class every discrepancy, and resolve it.** Where a mutant's failing set differs from its declared set, the difference is one of six things, and the report says which: **an extra failure** (a test failed that the row did not name); **a missing predicted failure** (a named test passed); **a surviving mutant** (nothing failed: the behaviour has no test that can fail); **a collection failure** (the suite did not collect or import, so the run says nothing about the row); **a timeout** (the run reached its limit and was killed, so it says nothing about the row); or **an unrelated crash** (a failure that does not come from the edit: a flaky test, a full disk). None is resolved quietly: the declared set stays in the report beside the observed one, and no test is changed merely so that the two agree. An extra or a missing failure is traced to its cause. Either the prediction was mistaken, in which case the report says why and gives the corrected expectation as a correction, beside the original declaration; or the plan and the implementation really differ. **A correction of an expected set stays in this task. A fix to code or to tests does not**: it reopens the task that owns the code, where it is made with that task's cycle and gates (its red and green steps, the full local gate, and the site build and both checks where that task requires them), and, for any clip whose look or timing it changes, Task 13's Steps 1 to 3 are made again for that clip (its sheet redrawn and looked at, its caption and description read against it) before the build of Task 13, Step 4 and the browser checks of Steps 5 and 6 (Firefox and Safari included) that the change affects, which are made again for any clip, page or stylesheet it changes; then the mutation run starts again from a clean baseline pinned to the new head, its collected IDs recorded afresh, the declaration checked against them, and every row run again, since a new test or a changed line can change any row's set. A surviving mutant gets a test, which is a fix and reopens its task as above, or a stated reason why it has none. A collection failure or an unrelated crash is run again once its cause is removed. A timeout is traced like a discrepancy: if the edit itself makes the suite hang, the row is a fault of this declaration, and it is replaced by a bounded edit of the same behaviour or removed with a stated reason and the test that still pins that behaviour, each as a correction beside the original; if the hang does not come from the edit (a slow runner, a stuck tool), its cause is removed and the row run again.
- [ ] **Step 4: Report** the table of results with each row's class and resolution. **The run counts as evidence only when every row is either exact or resolved in one of these ways**; until then the hand-over's mutation line says "not complete" and lists what is open.

---

### Hand-over

The coordinator opens the pull request as a draft after Task 1 and, in its description, gives: Task 1's "Probe results" (the sheets, the timings here and on the runner, Quarto 1.11.9's items (a) to (h), the interpreter and the encoder's version line on the runner, the decode probe, and the format decision); the final contact sheets with Task 13's findings, the headless-Chrome numbers, and one line per browser for Firefox and Safari; the pre-render times of Tasks 8 and 10; the printed estimate of the re-recorded 2D archive; the mutation table of Task 14 with its declared and observed sets and, for each discrepancy, its class and how it was resolved (the run is evidence only once every row is exact or resolved: Task 14, Step 4); and a plain statement of what this pull request leaves as it was: the `demo-*` commands, which still write GIFs with the earlier animation code until pull request 4. **Before the merge.** The merge gate is Codex's verdict on the reviewed head together with green checks on that same head. After review converges and the pull request is marked ready, the coordinator finds, by the head's full SHA, the run that each merge-gating workflow started for it and confirms that each **concluded `success`** (a non-empty `conclusion` shows only that a run finished; `failure`, `cancelled` or `skipped` is not evidence): `CI` (the default and slow steps and the wheel check); `Site check` on Quarto 1.11.9, with `Figure contract OK: 10 pages, 18 figures checked.` in its log, the pre-render step's twelve `rendered` lines and the job's duration; and `Replay report`, all three jobs, each with `demo_2d: reproduced` or, if not, the report's first difference quoted in the pull request (the gate, not the report, decides). `Deploy site` (`site.yml`) runs only on a push to `main`, so it cannot run before the merge; `Site check` makes the same build (the same setup, `uv sync --frozen`, pre-render step and render) and is the pre-merge evidence for the deployment. Only then is the pull request merged. **After the merge.** On `main`, the `CI` run and the `Deploy site` run for the merge commit, found by its full SHA, are confirmed to have concluded `success`, and the deployed landing page and one story page are opened, in Safari among others: the poster shows, the clip plays and seeking works.

## Self-review

**Section 11, item 3, against the two plans.** In this plan: every clip drawn and timed, a clean build on Quarto 1.11.9, cold-runner timings (Task 1); `_video.py` and the encoder dependency (Task 2); the clip builders (Tasks 3 to 6); the 2D truth with its archive, clip, story cell and the box-margin guard (Task 11); the pre-render script (Tasks 8 and 10); embeds and descriptions (Task 10); clip support in the check (Task 7) and in the guard (Tasks 9 and 10); the provenance prose about the site (Tasks 10 and 12); removal of the GIFs (Task 10); "if drawing shows the archive lacks something" (Task 1, Step 7). In `docs/plans/2026-11-24-replay-commands.md`: the demo commands, the migration note, the version, the wheel check, the prose about the commands, and removal of `_animate.py`, the old panel functions and their tests.

**Obligations elsewhere in the design.** Section 3: `EXPECTED_VISUAL_HASHES` loses the rest of its rows and the calibration report gains its dated note (Task 10). Section 4: rebuild on change, the encoder's path and version printed, the lookup order, pixel sizes asserted, native controls, one description per clip, the stylesheet rule, in a file of its own rather than in `styles.css` (decision D45) (Tasks 2, 3, 7, 8, 10). Section 5: every convention and each of the six clips (Tasks 3 to 6), drawn before any layout number is final (Task 1). Section 7: wholly in the commands plan, except that `render_clip` joins `clocks.viz` here, when it first exists. Section 8: every bullet (Task 11). Section 9: the replay-data paragraph, the echolocation and model-comparison sentences, the CI paragraph (Task 10); "run exactly once" (Task 12); the sentence about what the commands write is true to this head here and rewritten in the commands plan. Section 10: the clip rows of the manifest, the clip checks, the decoded stream, the manifest against the source, retired assets (Tasks 7, 9, 10); the path filter already holds `assets/replays/**` and the study JSON. "Testing": the clip tests (Tasks 2 to 6 and 8), the site fixtures (Task 7), the pinned sentences and descriptions (Tasks 10 to 12); `tests/test_cli.py` is the commands plan's. "Mutation checks": Task 14. "Checks by eye": Tasks 1 and 13.

**The state between the two pull requests, sentence by sentence.** At this plan's head the seven commands are untouched: six write GIFs through `clocks._animate`, `demo-density` writes its PNG, `--output` is their option, `OUTPUT_PATH` constants and `tests/test_cli.py`'s expectations stand, `clocks.viz` exports the old names beside `render_clip`, and `tests/test_viz.py` passes. What this plan removes is only what the site showed: the twelve GIF files, their hash rows (which become retired-file rows), the README's image and its paragraph about committed media. What it leaves saying "GIF" is true: the README's and Getting Started's command lists (the retired-asset guard allows `output/<name>.gif`, a command's own output path); the README's paragraph on what the commands animate; and two sentences this plan adds and pins, one in the README and one on the Reproducibility page, that the commands still write GIFs drawn by the earlier animation code. No task here uses `--output-dir`, `--theme`, `--video`, `write_outputs` or any other name the commands plan creates; the commands plan deletes the two pinned sentences when it makes them false.

**Names across tasks.** `Clip.show(row, azimuth=None)`, `Shot(row, repeat, azimuth)`, `shots`, `build_clip`, `final_frame`, `render_clip(replay, path, *, theme, encoder) -> int`, `frame_rgba`, `encode(frames, path, *, width, height, encoder) -> int`, `_run_encoder`, `_run_decoder`, `probe -> StreamInfo`, `parse_progress`, `dwell_frames`, `find_encoder`, `CLIP_HEIGHTS`, `BUILDERS`, `CLIP_DEMOS`, `STAGED`, `render_site_media(archives, media, *, full, demos, sources, report)`, `REPLAY_CSS`, `_read_poster(path) -> (size, problem)`, `_site_path(src, page_path, output_dir, what)`, `_links_the_replay_stylesheet(hrefs, page_path, output_dir)`, `Embed`, `REPLAY_EMBEDS`, `_embed_block`, `Fig("clip", height, name)`, and the tables `DOTS`, `ELLIPSES`, `TRUTHS`, `INSETS`, `ARTISTS` are used with these signatures wherever they appear. Inset copies are named `"<inset gid>-<artist gid>"` in Tasks 3 and 4.

**Each task's tests at its own commit.** Tasks 3 to 6 parametrize over `BUILDERS`, so a clip's shared tests appear with its builder. Task 7's fixtures need Task 2's `probe` and a demo's `record()` only, and its media-set check passes on the real site because no build ships media before Task 10. Task 8's tests call the function directly. Task 9's page tests are vacuous until Task 10 fills the table. Task 10's description test reads the 0.15 archive, and Task 11 changes archive and text together.

**The site after each task that touches it.** Tasks 7 to 9: pages and build unchanged. Task 10: the pre-render step and the media resources are switched on in the same commit as the embeds, the manifest and the guard; the GIFs go; the prose is true. Task 11: the 2D clip, descriptions and cell agree with the new truth. Task 12: prose only. At no commit does a page name a file that is not there or a build ship a media file that no page shows.

## Probe results

Task 1, recorded at `7338965` (the probe script `docs/plans/2026-11-19-replay-and-animation/clip_probe.py` and the seven sheets beside it). Run here on macOS arm64, Python 3.14.0, NumPy 2.4.2, Matplotlib 3.10.8, Quarto 1.9.32; on the runner by a temporary workflow on a throwaway branch (deleted afterwards), run <https://github.com/jbwhit/clocks/actions/runs/45456816717>, head `3363e0e` (the probe commit `7338965` plus the workflow file). The run concludes `failure` because its last step, the Quarto build, exits 1 on item (c); every earlier step succeeded.

**Verdict of Step 7: stop.** Item (c) fails on Quarto 1.11.9 (and on 1.9.32 here). Every other item, the encoder, the format and the time hold. Details below.

**The runner's environment.** `uv run --frozen` gives `3.12.16 (main, Nov 11 2026, 00:15:59) [Clang 23.1.3 ]`, a 3.12 as the site jobs assume (decision D42: no finding). Encoder: `imageio-ffmpeg` 0.8.1's `ffmpeg-linux-x86_64-v9.0.2`, version line `ffmpeg version n9.0.2-17-g2a571b6068-20260930 Copyright (c) 2000-2026 the FFmpeg developers`; it runs, encodes `libx264` and passes the decode probe. Here: `ffmpeg-macos-aarch64-v9.0`, `ffmpeg version 9.0 Copyright (c) 2000-2026 the FFmpeg developers`.

**Quarto, items (a) to (h).** Measured: the same outcome on both versions.

| Item | 1.9.32 (here) | 1.11.9 (runner) |
|---|---|---|
| (a) pre-render in the project directory, under the starting interpreter (same `sys.prefix`); `QUARTO_PROJECT_RENDER_ALL=1` on both full renders, unset on the single-page render | holds | holds (Python 3.12.16) |
| (b) `_output/media/` holds the four files after a clean render | holds | holds |
| (c) the two `<video>` lines, the `<figure class="replay">` line and the `<figcaption>` line unchanged | **fails** | **fails** |
| (d) one `id="demo-2d-text"` holding the div's text, on each page | holds | holds |
| (e) `<body>` has `quarto-light`; `site_libs` hides `.light-content` under `body.quarto-dark` and `.dark-content` under `body.quarto-light` | holds | holds |
| (f) `_output/replay.css` byte for byte the source; both pages link it (`replay.css`, `../replay.css`), after `styles.css` | holds | holds |
| (g) the render whose pre-render script exits 1 exits non-zero | holds | holds |
| (h) `output-only.mp4` gone after the second full render | holds | holds |

**The stop is resolved by amendment** (the coordinator's ruling, made after this section was written): the rendered page is compared as Quarto writes it, with no change to the format and no Task 1b. Decision D22 now says what Quarto changes, and Task 7's check and fixtures require the figure's classes to be exactly `replay` and `figure` and accept a boolean attribute bare or as `=""`. The model-comparison connectors and the two-mass labels noted at the end of this section are settled by decisions D50 and D51.

How (c) fails, identically on both versions and on both pages: Quarto's HTML post-processing rewrites the raw block. The `<figure>` gains a class, `class="replay figure"`; on each `<video>` the bare attributes become `controls=""` and `playsinline=""`. Every other attribute keeps its value and its order, the `src` and `poster` paths are untouched (`media/…` on the root page, `../media/…` in `story/`), and the `<figcaption>` line arrives unchanged. Pandoc may pass the block through, but the page Quarto writes is not byte for byte the block (decision D22's premise). What this touches downstream, for the coordinator: D22's statement and Task 10's guard (the source still holds the canonical block; only the rendered page differs); Task 7's parser, whose `_Video` attributes treat a bare `controls` as present with the value `None`, while the rendered page has `""`; and any rendered-site rule that compares the figure's class list for equality rather than membership (`replay` is still in it). Also recorded: `source-only.mp4` was copied to `_output/media/` by the second full render, as expected; and after the failing render (g) `_output/` does not exist at all, on both versions (a full render whose pre-render step fails leaves no site, not the previous one).

**Decode probe** (Step 3's small clips), here and on the runner; exit status, first `Video:` line, last `frame=` and `progress=` lines.

- Three solid frames of 64 by 48 written 2, 1 and 4 times: exit 0, `Video: h264 (High) (avc1 / 0x31637661), yuv420p(progressive), 64x48, 19 kb/s, 20 fps, 20 tbr, 10240 tbn (default)`, `frame=7`, `progress=end`; boxes `ftyp moov free mdat`; 1,793 bytes here, 1,798 on the runner. Both builds.

The probe job took 3 min 5 s on the runner (job 18:11:40 to 18:14:45 UTC); its timing step 2 min 32 s.

**Time (Step 7).** t = 151.6 s on the runner, between 120 s and 240 s, so the sum rule applies: the latest `Site check` run (<https://github.com/jbwhit/clocks/actions/runs/45339815562>, job `render` 06:21:16 to 06:22:45 UTC) took 89 s; 89 + 151.6 = 240.6 s, under the 480 s that are eight of the job's ten minutes. Time alone would let the work go on. The design expected under two minutes; it is 2.5.

**The format (Step 7).** Every panel was drawn from the keys `validate_replay` accepts, without importing `clocks.physics` or `clocks.inference` and without running a demo (the one `record()` call is the 2D run at 0.10 that Task 11 makes, done in memory for its sheet). No sheet shows a panel misleading for want of data: the 500 dots show the two-mass map's second mode (row 20: a dense lump of mass 2 near (0.5, 3) beside the sparse spread around its truth, which the ellipse cannot show); the insets show no float32 steps. The format holds.

**The slowing panel (Step 7, decision D19).** The prior's bars are in view at k = 0 and do not flatten the later ones: including the prior row widens the y-range by 14% (`demo_1d`, −0.0011 to 0.0508 against 0.0050 to 0.0506 without it) and by 5% (`demo_multi_mass`). At 1500 pixels the predicted bars are 119 to 131 pixels long at k = 0, 11 to 47 at k = 1, 4 to 17 at k = 8 and 2 to 7 at the last row (the observed bar 47, 17 and 7); without the prior row the last would be 2 to 8. The last row's bars are short because the final uncertainty is small against the spread of slowing between clocks, not because of the prior. D19 stands.

**Layout values.** `LAYOUTS`, `SIDE_OFFSET = 0.5` and `GUTTER = 22.0` as in the script at `7338965`. Every figure-level rectangle lies in the unit square and no two overlap; the echolocation whole-prior box sits at upper left beside the scene; in both track clips `strip` and `slowing` have the cloud's left edge (0.085) and width (0.50).

**Drawing details settled by the sheets that `LAYOUTS` does not carry** (Tasks 3 to 6 need them to reproduce what was looked at): each inset sits flush with the top of its panel, and its zoom label is the inset's text placed just above it (`(0, 1.03)` in inset fractions, `clip_on=False`), so no label lies over data; the key `covariance, 2 s.d.` is the panel's text just above the panel's top right (`(1, 1.015)`; centred over the two-mass track cloud), and `stronger fill = slower clock` sits below the 2D map at its right (`(1, −0.085)`), with the map's tick labels padded 5 points; the `mass 1` and `mass 2` labels are placed, once, after the final row is shown, at whichever of eight compass offsets (9 and 7 points) covers the fewest final marks without leaving the panel or touching an inset (a fixed offset put them on the final clouds); the two-mass map's trace headers are 8.5 points (9.5 elsewhere), since at 9.5 the right column's headers left the canvas; the close view has `set_box_aspect((1, 1, 1), zoom=1.1)`, ticks at the even integers inside `view_bounds`, panes in `background` with `rule` edges; the fit panel's `no readings yet` sits at its top left, off the diagonal; the model clip's x ticks stop at T and its bottom spine is bounded at T.

**Decisions D17, D18 and D20: unchanged.** D17's singular and D18's label are drawn as written (the labels read, for example, `zoom ×21 (x), ×8 (M)` and `zoom ×8 (x), ×6 (y)`); D20's rectangles are the probe's `LAYOUTS`.

**Seen on the sheets but not a Step 2 item, for the coordinator.** In the model clip, a connector runs from a line's head to its label in the gutter, so at early rows each connector crosses the whole empty panel and reads like a projection of the line (thinned to 0.7 points at alpha 0.6 here; still visible). And at rows 0 and 1 the two-mass labels, which sit beside their truths inside the cloud panel, lie over prior dots, as any label inside a panel full of the prior would.
